**Options to show more data:**

1. **Increase limit** — change to top 1000 or 5000 in export.py
2. **Use pagination** — load data in chunks as user scrolls
3. **Use full file** — change fetch to `/data/applications.json` (may be slow, ~5MB)

# Architecture Audit Report

## What Was Planned vs What Was Done

### ML Pipeline

| Component | Planned | Implemented | Status |
|-----------|---------|-------------|--------|
| **extract.py** | Read Excel, parse dates/codes | ✅ Done | Working |
| **transform.py** | Feature engineering, dedup | ✅ Done | Working |
| **score.py** | Impact Score, SHAP values | ⚠️ Partial | No SHAP, simplified scoring |
| **export.py** | JSON export for frontend | ✅ Done | Working |
| **train.py** | XGBoost models | ❌ Missing | Not implemented |
| **simulate.py** | Pre-compute simulations | ⚠️ Partial | Basic only |
| **fraud_detection** | Isolation Forest + HDBSCAN | ❌ Missing | Only rule-based flags |

### Frontend Data Flow

| Component | Planned | Implemented | Status |
|-----------|---------|-------------|--------|
| **Data Layer** ([lib/data/index.ts](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/lib/data/index.ts:0:0-0:0)) | API abstraction | ⚠️ **Broken** | Returns empty mock data |
| **ScoringTable** | Fetch from API/data layer | ⚠️ Hacky | Direct fetch from static JSON |
| **Applications JSON** | Full dataset (36K records) | ✅ Exists | 47MB file in [lib/data/](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/lib/data:0:0-0:0) |
| **Top 500 JSON** | Quick loading subset | ✅ Exists | 769KB in `public/data/` |

### API Endpoints

| Endpoint | Planned | Implemented | Status |
|----------|---------|-------------|--------|
| **/api/score** | Real-time scoring | ✅ Done | Working, but simplified logic |
| **/api/simulate** | Budget simulation | ✅ Done | Working |
| **/api/explain** | SHAP explanations | ⚠️ Stub | No real SHAP values |
| **/api/chat** | LLM assistant | ❌ Missing | Not implemented |

---

## Critical Issues Found

### 1. **Broken Data Layer Architecture** 🔴

`@/Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/lib/data/index.ts` returns **empty mock arrays**:

```typescript
const MOCK_APPLICATIONS: Application[] = [];  // Empty!
```

The [ScoringTable](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/components/islands/ScoringTable.tsx:103:0-459:1) bypasses this entirely and fetches directly from [/data/applications-top500.json](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/public/data/applications-top500.json:0:0-0:0). This is why only 500 records are shown.

### 2. **Data Duplication** 🟡

Same data exists in two places:
- [src/lib/data/applications.json](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/lib/data/applications.json:0:0-0:0) (47MB, all 36K records) — **not used**
- [public/data/applications-top500.json](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/public/data/applications-top500.json:0:0-0:0) (769KB, 500 records) — **used by table**

### 3. **No Server-Side Data Access** 🔴

The architecture doc planned Cloudflare KV for data, but current implementation:
- Uses static JSON files
- No pagination API
- No server-side filtering
- Frontend loads entire dataset into memory

### 4. **Missing ML Components** 🟡

- No XGBoost training (using heuristic scoring instead)
- No SHAP values (explain API returns fake data)
- No fraud detection ML (only rule-based flags)

---

## The Core Problem

**The current architecture is a hackathon demo, not production-ready.**

For production with government users needing all 36K+ records:

| Current | Production Needed |
|---------|-------------------|
| Static JSON files | Database (KV/D1/external) |
| Client-side filtering | Server-side pagination API |
| Load 500 records | Load on-demand with pagination |
| No auth | Role-based access |
| Simplified scoring | Real ML models |

---

## Recommended Production Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     PRODUCTION ARCHITECTURE                   │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                               │
│  Frontend (Astro + Solid.js)                                 │
│       │                                                       │
│       ▼                                                       │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │
│  │              API Layer (Cloudflare Workers)          │     │
│  │  GET /api/applications?page=1&limit=50&oblast=...   │     │
│  │  POST /api/score                                     │     │
│  │  POST /api/simulate                                  │     │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │
│       │                                                       │
│       ▼                                                       │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │
│  │           Data Layer (Cloudflare D1 / KV)            │     │
│  │  • All 36K applications in database                  │     │
│  │  • Server-side filtering, sorting, pagination        │     │
│  │  • Pre-computed aggregates cached in KV              │     │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │
│                                                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

# Full Architecture Audit Complete

Created `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/docs/ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md` with complete findings.

## Summary

### What Was Planned
- ML Pipeline: Extract → Transform → Train (XGBoost) → Score (SHAP) → Export
- Frontend: Data layer abstraction → API calls → Cloudflare KV storage
- All 36,651 records accessible via paginated API

### What Was Done
| Layer | Status | Issue |
|-------|--------|-------|
| **ML Pipeline** | ✅ Working | Heuristic scoring instead of trained XGBoost |
| **Data Layer** | ❌ Broken | [lib/data/index.ts](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/lib/data/index.ts:0:0-0:0) returns empty arrays |
| **ScoringTable** | ⚠️ Hacky | Direct fetch bypasses data layer, loads only 500 |
| **Full Dataset** | ⚠️ Unused | 47MB [applications.json](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/lib/data/applications.json:0:0-0:0) exists but never loaded |
| **Cloudflare KV** | ❌ Not used | Planned but not implemented |
| **SHAP values** | ❌ Fake | Returns canned explanations |
| **Chat API** | ❌ Missing | Not implemented |

### Root Cause of 500 Limit
```
export.py creates applications-top500.json with limit=500
    ↓
Copied to public/data/ for direct HTTP access
    ↓
ScoringTable.tsx fetches this directly (bypassing broken data layer)
    ↓
Dashboard shows only 500 records
```

The full 36K dataset ([applications.json](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/lib/data/applications.json:0:0-0:0), 47MB) **exists but is never used**.

### Was It Done Right?
- **ML Pipeline:** ✅ Yes, clean architecture
- **Frontend Data Flow:** ❌ No, fundamentally broken
- **API Layer:** ⚠️ Partial, basic endpoints work

## Arguments Against Nuking Each Option

### 1. Keep ML Pipeline (Don't nuke it)

**What works well:**
- [extract.py](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/ml/extract.py:0:0-0:0) correctly parses 36,651 records from Excel with proper normalization
- [transform.py](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/ml/transform.py:0:0-0:0) creates 50+ features including retry detection, HHI concentration, fraud indicators
- [score.py](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/ml/score.py:0:0-0:0) has a solid 6-component weighted scoring formula
- [export.py](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/ml/export.py:0:0-0:0) handles NaN values and generates valid JSON

**Why keep it:**
- ~800 lines of tested, working Python code
- Feature engineering logic is complex and took time to get right
- The scoring formula is documented and interpretable
- Rebuilding would just recreate the same logic

**Counter-argument (why nuke it):**
- No actual ML training (XGBoost, SHAP are missing)
- Heuristic scoring, not data-driven
- If you want real ML, it needs major changes anyway

---

### 2. Rebuild Everything (Don't nuke everything)

**What you'd lose:**
- All the domain knowledge encoded in code (oblast mapping, subsidy categorization, fraud indicators)
- Working API endpoint logic ([score.ts](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/pages/api/score.ts:0:0-0:0), [simulate.ts](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/pages/api/simulate.ts:0:0-0:0))
- UI components that work (map, simulator, precheck form)
- ~3000+ lines of code across Python and TypeScript

**Why keep something:**
- Starting from scratch takes 3-5x longer than fixing what's broken
- The broken parts are *isolated* (just the data layer)
- UI design is already polished

**Counter-argument (why nuke it):**
- "Fixing" broken architecture often leads to more patches
- Clean slate = clean architecture from day one
- Faster to build right than debug wrong

---

### 3. Keep Data Only (Don't nuke processed data)

**What the data gives you:**
- [scored_df.pkl](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/ml/data/scored_df.pkl:0:0-0:0) - 36,651 records with all features calculated
- [aggregates.pkl](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/ml/data/aggregates.pkl:0:0-0:0) - Pre-computed statistics
- Hours of ML pipeline execution saved

**Why keep it:**
- You can immediately build frontend against real data
- No need to re-run extract/transform/score
- Data is correct, only delivery mechanism is broken

**Counter-argument (why nuke it):**
- Pickle files are fragile (Python version dependent)
- JSON files are bloated (47MB)
- Fresh export with better format (e.g., SQLite) might be better

---

## My Recommendation

**Keep the ML pipeline + data, rebuild only the frontend.**

Reason: The problem is specifically in [agroscore/src/lib/data/index.ts](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/lib/data/index.ts:0:0-0:0) and [ScoringTable.tsx](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/components/islands/ScoringTable.tsx:0:0-0:0) - about 300 lines of broken code. The other ~4000 lines work fine.

But if you want a clean architecture with proper testing from the start, rebuilding the frontend layer-by-layer is reasonable. The ML pipeline doesn't need to change.

What's your preference?

### Planner Response

You're right to be skeptical. Here's what I actually found:

## ML Pipeline Issues

### 1. **Score Does NOT Predict Outcomes**
| Status | Mean Score | Count |
|--------|-----------|-------|
| Approved | 62.34 | 31,481 |
| Rejected | 62.14 | 2,909 |
| **Difference** | **0.20 points** | — |

A 0.2 point difference is meaningless. The score doesn't predict rejection.

### 2. **High Scores Get Rejected, Low Scores Get Approved**
- **Rank #6** (score 86.6) → **REJECTED**
- **All bottom 10** (scores 39-44) → **APPROVED**

### 3. **It's Not ML, It's Just Heuristics**
The "ML pipeline" doesn't train anything:
- No XGBoost model training
- No learning from historical approvals/rejections
- Just a weighted formula based on assumptions

### What This Means

The scoring formula is **made up**, not data-driven. It assumes:
- "Breeding" category = high priority
- Small farmers = bonus
- Round numbers = fraud risk

But these assumptions aren't validated against actual outcomes.

**Conclusion:** If you want real ML that predicts which applications will be approved/rejected, the pipeline needs to be rebuilt with actual model training.

## Current GISS (FIFO) System Schema

Based on the Excel export from `subsidy.plem.kz`:

```sql
-- What the government system likely has (reconstructed)
CREATE TABLE applications (
    id              SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    app_num         VARCHAR(14) UNIQUE,      -- '01300100258082'
    submission_date TIMESTAMP,               -- FIFO ordering key
    oblast          VARCHAR(50),             -- 'область Абай'
    akimat          VARCHAR(200),            -- government office
    direction       VARCHAR(100),            -- 'Субсидирование в скотоводстве'
    subsidy_name    TEXT,                    -- full program name
    status          VARCHAR(50),             -- Получена → Одобрена → Исполнена
    norm            DECIMAL(10,2),           -- rate per unit (15000 tenge/head)
    sum             DECIMAL(15,2),           -- total amount (2640000 tenge)
    district        VARCHAR(100)             -- 'район Ақсуат'
);

-- FIFO Logic: ORDER BY submission_date ASC, allocate until budget = 0
```

**Key insight:** The current system has **no scoring**. It's pure queue-based.

## Comparison: Website vs Excel

| # | Website (subsidy.plem.kz) | Excel (our dataset) | Match? |
|---|---------------------------|---------------------|--------|
| 1 | № | № п/п | ✅ |
| 2 | Дата подачи заявки | Дата поступления | ✅ |

| 5 | Номер заявки | Номер заявки | ✅ |
| 6 | Статус заявки | Статус заявки | ✅ |
| 7 | Направление субсидирования | Наименование субсидирования | ✅ |
| 8 | Управление сельского хозяйства | Акимат | ✅ |
| 9 | Сумма субсидии | Причитающая сумма | ✅ |
| 10 | — | Норматив | 🟡 Not on web |
| 11 | — | Район хозяйства | 🟡 Not on web |
| 12 | Решение по заявке (rejection reason) | ❌ MISSING | 🔴 **Not in Excel** |

### Key Differences

1. **Applicant names removed**: Website shows "ТОО 'Ен-Дала'" (company name), Excel is anonymized ("обезлич" = de-identified)

2. **Rejection reasons not in Excel**: Image 1 shows detailed rejection text like:
   > "Согласно пункта 21 Правил... Вам отказано в оказании государственной услуги... Причина отказа: Документ электронная копия акта карантинирования у продавца (при импорте) не загружается..."

3. **95,472 records on website** vs **36,651 in our Excel** — website has more data (likely multi-year)

### For Our Schema

The website shows the **actual producer-level data**. Our Excel is a subset that's been anonymized. We should design our schema to match the website structure but acknowledge we only have anonymized data:

```sql
CREATE TABLE applications (
    id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    app_num         TEXT UNIQUE,         -- Номер заявки
    submission_date DATETIME,            -- Дата подачи заявки
    oblast          TEXT,                -- Область
    applicant_name  TEXT,                -- Наименование заявителя (anonymized in our data)
    akimat          TEXT,                -- Управление сельского хозяйства
    subsidy_name    TEXT,                -- Направление субсидирования
    status          TEXT,                -- Статус заявки
    sum             REAL,                -- Сумма субсидии
    norm            REAL,                -- Норматив (from Excel only)
    district        TEXT,                -- Район хозяйства (from Excel only)
    rejection_reason TEXT                -- Решение по заявке (not in our Excel)
);
```

### Planner Response

Good question. Here are three approaches:

## Option 1: Abstract Data Source

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    DATA ADAPTER                          │
│                                                          │
│   ExcelAdapter          APIAdapter (future)             │
│   ├─ read_excel()       ├─ fetch_from_giss()            │
│   ├─ anonymized=True    ├─ has_applicant_names          │
│   └─ 36K records        └─ 95K+ records                 │
│                                                          │
│            ↓ Both output same schema ↓                  │
│                                                          │
│   applications (                                         │
│     app_num, submission_date, oblast, status,           │
│     sum, norm, district,                                │
│     applicant_id,  -- hash or real name                 │
│     is_anonymized  -- flag                              │
│   )                                                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Pros:** Clean separation, easy to swap data source  
**Cons:** Need to maintain two adapters

---

## Option 2: Use `producer_id` as Pseudo-Identity

We already extract `producer_id` from `app_num` (first 11 digits):
```
app_num:     01300100258082
producer_id: 01300100258  ← This is consistent per applicant
```

This lets us:
- Track same applicant across multiple applications
- Calculate retry patterns
- Detect fraud clusters

**Without knowing the actual company name.**

---

## Option 3: Design for Real Data, Test with Anonymized

Build schema assuming we'll eventually get real data:
```sql
CREATE TABLE applications (
    -- Core fields (both sources)
    app_num         TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    submission_date DATETIME,
    oblast          TEXT,
    status          TEXT,
    sum             REAL,
    
    -- Applicant (real or derived)
    applicant_id    TEXT,           -- producer_id from app_num
    applicant_name  TEXT DEFAULT NULL,  -- NULL for anonymized
    
    -- Extra fields
    district        TEXT,           -- from Excel
    rejection_reason TEXT DEFAULT NULL  -- from website/API
);
```

When loading:
- **Excel data:** `applicant_id` = derived from `app_num`, `applicant_name` = NULL
- **Real data:** `applicant_id` = company ID, `applicant_name` = "ТОО 'Ен-Дала'"

---

*Asked user a question*

### Planner Response

## Arguments FOR Each Option

---

### **Option 3: Future-proof schema**

**Why choose this:**

1. **Minimal extra work now** — Just add `applicant_name TEXT DEFAULT NULL`. If it's NULL, UI shows "Anonymized" or uses `producer_id`.

2. **Smooth upgrade path** — When/if you get GISS API access, you just populate the `applicant_name` field. No schema migration needed.

3. **The derived `producer_id` already works** — We proved it tracks the same applicant across applications. 23,851 unique producers identified from 36,651 applications.

4. **Demo-ready** — For the hackathon, showing "Producer #01300100258" is fine. Real names can come later.

**Best for:** Getting something working fast while keeping options open.

---

### **Option 1: Adapter pattern**

**Why choose this:**

1. **Clean architecture** — Each data source has its own loader. Business logic doesn't care where data comes from.

2. **Testable** — You can unit test each adapter independently. Mock the GISS adapter until you have real access.

3. **Multiple data sources simultaneously** — Could load Excel + scrape website + future API all into same database.

4. **Follows established patterns** — This is how production ETL systems work. Shows engineering maturity to judges.

**Best for:** If you want to demonstrate software engineering best practices, not just ML.

---

### **Option: Excel only for now**

**Why choose this:**

1. **YAGNI (You Ain't Gonna Need It)** — Hackathon is about demonstrating the *concept*. Real GISS integration is a government procurement project, not a weekend hack.

2. **36K records is enough** — Proves the scoring system works. Scaling to 95K is trivial if the algorithm is right.

3. **Fewer moving parts** — Every abstraction layer is a potential bug. Ship something that works.

4. **Focus on the ML** — The differentiation is your scoring algorithm and explainability, not the data pipeline.

**Best for:** Maximum output with minimum complexity. Ship fast.

---

### **Option: Need GISS API access first**

**Why choose this:**

1. **Don't build on assumptions** — The website might have rate limits, CAPTCHAs, or require government credentials. Building an adapter for something you can't access is wasted work.

2. **The Excel IS official data** — It came from GISS. The 2025-2026 export is actually more recent than the 2024 website data (95K records are older).

3. **API might not exist** — Government systems often have no public API. The website might be the only interface.

4. **Scope creep risk** — "Let's also integrate with GISS" is how projects go from 3 hours to 3 weeks.

**Best for:** Being realistic about what's achievable in your timeframe.

---

## My Recommendation

**Excel only for now** (Option 3's schema, but don't overthink the adapter).

```sql
CREATE TABLE applications (
    app_num TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    submission_date DATETIME,
    oblast TEXT,
    district TEXT,
    status TEXT,
    subsidy_name TEXT,
    sum REAL,
    norm REAL,
    producer_id TEXT,  -- derived from app_num
    -- Future fields, NULL for now:
    applicant_name TEXT,
    rejection_reason TEXT
);
```

This takes 5 minutes and keeps all doors open. Ready to proceed with the plan?

### User Input

if we choose option 3 how can we scale for real production? or you think we would overengineer it and might not even meet the real database needs

### Planner Response

Honest answer: **you're right to worry.** Here's why:

## The Over-Engineering Risk

We don't know what the real GISS database looks like internally. We're guessing from a web page and an Excel export. In production:

1. **We probably wouldn't own the data** — The Ministry of Agriculture owns GISS. Our system would be a *layer on top*, not a replacement.

2. **The real integration would be:**
```
GISS Database (theirs)
    ↓ API or data feed
Our Scoring Engine
    ↓ adds scores
Our Dashboard (reads from both)
```

3. **Our schema would only store what WE compute:**
```sql
-- We DON'T duplicate GISS data
-- We only store our additions
CREATE TABLE scores (
    app_num TEXT PRIMARY KEY,  -- FK to GISS
    impact_score REAL,
    fraud_risk REAL,
    score_components JSON,
    top_factors JSON,
    scored_at DATETIME
);
```

## Scaling Scenarios

| Scenario | What Happens |
|----------|-------------|
| **Hackathon demo** | We own everything (Excel → SQLite → Dashboard) |
| **Pilot with ministry** | We read from GISS export, store only scores |
| **Full production** | GISS sends webhook/API → we score in real-time → scores stored in our DB, applications stay in GISS |

## The Honest Truth

Designing a "production schema" now is **premature optimization**. We don't know:
- How GISS would expose data to us (API? CSV dump? Direct DB access?)
- What fields they'd provide beyond the Excel export
- Whether they'd even let a third party access their database
- What their internal IDs look like

## What Actually Matters for the Hackathon

The judges care about:
1. **Does the scoring algorithm work?** (ML quality)
2. **Can you explain why an application scored high/low?** (Explainability)
3. **Does the dashboard show useful insights?** (UX)
4. **Is the approach feasible to integrate?** (Architecture story)

They don't care about our database schema. They care about the **concept** and **demo**.

---

## My Updated Recommendation

**Don't design a schema at all.** Keep it dead simple:

```
Excel → pandas DataFrame → score → export to JSON → frontend reads JSON
```

For the demo, this works. For production, the schema would be dictated by whoever integrates with GISS — not us.

What we should focus energy on:
- **Make the scoring actually ML-driven** (XGBoost, not heuristics)
- **Make SHAP explanations real**
- **Make the dashboard show all records cleanly**

*Asked user a question*

### Planner Response

Yes, **if the architecture is layered correctly.** Here's why:

## What Matters for Adaptability

The scoring logic and dashboard are **independent of where the data comes from.** If you win:

```
NOW (Hackathon Demo):
  Excel file → Python scoring → JSON → Frontend

AFTER (Production Integration):
  GISS API/DB → Same Python scoring → Same Frontend
                 ↑
          Only this adapter changes
```

The key parts that transfer directly:
- **Scoring algorithm** (XGBoost model, weights, features) — 100% reusable
- **SHAP explanations** — 100% reusable
- **Dashboard UI** — 100% reusable
- **Data loading** — **this is the only part that changes**

## What Changes in Production

| Component | Hackathon | Production |
|-----------|-----------|------------|
| Data input | `pd.read_excel()` | `giss_api.fetch()` or DB connector |
| Data storage | JSON files | Their database + our scores table |
| Scoring | Same XGBoost model | Same model, retrained on more data |
| Frontend | Same dashboard | Same dashboard |

**One function changes.** Everything else stays.

## The Rule

As long as your scoring function takes a DataFrame and returns scores:

```python
def score(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    # This doesn't care where df came from
    # Excel, API, database — same input shape, same output
```

It doesn't matter what the source is. The ministry provides the data in whatever format they use, you adapt the **input adapter only**, and everything downstream works.

---

**Bottom line:** Build the demo with Excel → JSON. If you win, swapping the data source is a 1-day task, not a rewrite. The ML model and dashboard are the hard parts — and those are what you're building now.

### Planner Response

Good — now I've read the actual task brief. Here's what matters for our ML pipeline:

## Key Requirements from the Task Brief

1. **Must use real AI/ML** — "не просто фильтрации или поиска по ключевым словам"
2. **Explainability is mandatory** — "почему система пришла к такому выводу"
3. **AI assists, doesn't replace** — "помогать, а не заменять эксперта"
4. **Predictive models valued** — "предиктивные модели на реальных данных"
5. **Scalable + Good UX** for government officials

## What ML Can We Actually Do With This Data?

We have 36,651 applications with a known outcome (`status`). Three possible ML approaches:

### Approach A: Predict Rejection
- **Target:** `status == 'rejected'` (8% of records)
- **Question it answers:** "Will this application be rejected?"
- **Features:** amount, oblast, district, subsidy type, timing, retry patterns
- **Problem:** Imbalanced classes (92% approved). Also — we don't know *why* they were rejected (rejection reasons not in Excel).

### Approach B: Anomaly Detection (Fraud Risk)
- **Method:** Isolation Forest / autoencoders
- **Question it answers:** "Is this application suspicious?"
- **Features:** unusual amounts, clustering patterns, timing anomalies
- **Problem:** No ground truth — we don't know which are actually fraudulent.

### Approach C: Multi-Factor Priority Scoring
- **Method:** Learn weights from data (not make them up)
- **Question it answers:** "Which applications should be prioritized?"
- **Uses:** XGBoost trained on rejection patterns + SHAP to decompose score
- **Key insight:** Instead of predicting reject/approve, score = P(approval) weighted by efficiency factors

### My Take

**Approach C is the strongest for the hackathon.** Here's why:

- It combines prediction (ML) with domain logic (priority)
- SHAP gives real explainability
- It directly addresses the task: "distribute budgets fairly based on data, not FIFO order"
- The score helps the commission **prioritize**, not make final decisions

What do you think? Which approach resonates, or do you want to combine them?

# Full Docs Audit: For & Against

## 1. SCORING.md (1001 lines)

### What It Claims
- Predicting rejection is impossible (ceiling effect, 31% variance explained)
- Instead, use "Impact Score" to rank by potential impact
- Three-layer system: Pre-Check (rules) → Impact Score (ML) → Fairness (constraints)
- 6-component weighted formula (v2): Strategic, Fairness, Need, Efficiency, Mission, Fraud

### Arguments FOR ✅
- **Honest about limitations** — "69% variance unexplained" is rare for a hackathon. Judges will respect this.
- **Impact Score > Rejection Predictor** — The ceiling effect analysis is sound. Other teams WILL try to predict rejection and fail.
- **Pre-Check idea is valuable** — 65% retries is a real problem. Showing farmers errors before submission is genuinely useful.
- **The 3-layer approach maps to real users** — Pre-Check (farmer), Impact Score (commission), Fairness (analyst).

### Arguments AGAINST ❌
- **The formulas are made up** — Every weight, threshold, and coefficient is invented. "Calibrated on historical data" is a lie — there's no calibration step.
- **6 components is over-engineered** — The doc went from 4 to 6 components without proving the extra 2 add value. "Mission Alignment" overlaps heavily with "Strategic Alignment".
- **No validation** — The "backtesting" section shows expected results but never actually ran. The numbers (Gini 0.68→0.55) are fabricated targets.
- **Pre-Check rules require data we don't have** — The EligibilityChecker checks BIN, ISJ registration, cattle age, land — none of which exist in our Excel.

### Verdict: **Keep the philosophy (Impact > Rejection), simplify the formula (4 components max), drop Pre-Check rules we can't implement**

---

## 2. STRATEGY.md (622 lines)

### What It Claims
- 6 differentiation strategies + detailed storytelling
- Competitive analysis of what other teams will do
- Detailed 5-minute presentation structure
- Target score: 83-91/100

### Arguments FOR ✅
- **Simpson's Paradox is genuinely impressive** — This is a real finding from the data. Will differentiate from other teams.
- **Case-level dedup (9.2% → 3.7%)** — Genuine insight. Strong "aha moment" for judges.
- **Honest limitations slide** — Boldly admitting "69% invisible" builds trust.
- **The storytelling structure is good** — Hook → Problem → Insight → Solution → Demo → Results → Limitations.

### Arguments AGAINST ❌
- **Over-promises** — "Scrollytelling + particle flow" for 36K particles? Never implemented. Will waste time.
- **"MCP Server" is buzzword padding** — Adding Model Context Protocol to impress judges is transparent. Nobody is going to connect Claude to a hackathon project.
- **Estimated score 83-91 is delusional** — Without a working prototype, it's 0.
- **Too many differentiators** — 8 "MUST" items is too many. Should pick 3.

### Verdict: **Keep the storytelling structure and 3 key insights (Simpson's, case-level dedup, FIFO critique). Drop particle flow, MCP, scrollytelling fluff.**

### Planner Response

## Our 3 Solutions & Why

Based on the docs audit, everything distills into **3 core solutions** that directly address the hackathon case:

### Solution 1: Impact Score (Merit-based ranking)
**Problem it solves:** FIFO = whoever clicks "submit" first gets funded. No assessment of actual impact.

**Why it matters:** This is literally the hackathon task — "distribute budgets based on data, not submission order."

**How:** Train XGBoost on historical outcomes (approved/rejected/withdrawn). The model learns which features correlate with approval. SHAP values explain each score.

**Evidence from docs:**
- PATTERNS.md: district (0.058 bits), oblast (0.041), subsidy_code (0.032) — real predictive features
- SCORING.md: Impact Score philosophy is sound, but the heuristic formula was never validated
- RESEARCH.md: USDA EQIP does exactly this — merit-based ranking for agricultural subsidies

### Solution 2: Fraud Risk Flags
**Problem it solves:** 66B tenge wasted (Audit Chamber). Tyulkubas: 86% to one recipient.

**Why it matters:** Visible in our data — we can actually demonstrate this.

**How:** Isolation Forest for anomalies + rule-based flags (monopoly district, multi-district applicant, volume outliers).

**Evidence from docs:**
- AI-INTEGRATION.md: well-designed fraud pipeline (IF + rules)
- PATTERNS.md: monopolization and batch processing patterns verified
- Honest disclaimer: "fraud risk score, not fraud detection"

### Solution 3: Data Insights Dashboard
**Problem it solves:** Commission members have no visibility into distribution patterns.

**Why it matters:** Simpson's Paradox, case-level dedup (9.2%→3.7%), budget waves — genuine insights no one else will find.

**How:** Dashboard showing all 36K records + aggregate analytics.

**Evidence from docs:**
- PATTERNS.md: 11 verified patterns
- STRATEGY.md: these are the "aha moments" for the presentation

---

## Build Simple → Scale

```
Phase 1: MVP (~1 hour)
├── pipeline.py: Excel → Clean → Features → XGBoost → Score → JSON
├── Verify: model accuracy > random, scores make sense
└── Output: applications.json with scores

Phase 2: Explain + Detect (~1 hour)  
├── Add SHAP values per record
├── Add Isolation Forest anomaly scores
├── Add rule-based fraud flags
└── Output: enriched JSON with explanations + flags

Phase 3: Frontend (~1 hour)
├── Dashboard: paginated table, all 36K records
├── Filters: oblast, status, category
├── Score breakdown on row click (SHAP)
└── Fraud flags highlighted

Phase 4: Polish (if time allows)
├── Budget simulator (FIFO vs Merit comparison)
├── Aggregate stats by oblast/district
└── Presentation-ready charts
```

Each phase is **independently testable and demoable**. Phase 1 alone proves the ML works. Phase 2 adds explainability. Phase 3 makes it visual. Phase 4 is bonus.

### Planner Response

Excellent question. Let me think through how the current system actually works and where our solutions fit — or don't.

## How the Current System Actually Works

```
Jan-Mar:  New budget allocated → Applications flow in → FIFO processing → Most approved
Apr-Jul:  Budget depleting → Still processing → Reject rate rises
Aug:      Budget exhausted → 13.3% reject rate (peak)
Sep-Dec:  New tranche arrives? → Queue resumes
```

From our data:
- **2,854 applications** = approved by inspectors but waiting for money ("Сформировано поручение")
- **Akmola:** 50.7% of applications stuck in this queue
- **Pavlodar:** only 2.1% waiting — they have enough budget

### Why FIFO Isn't Entirely Wrong

| FIFO Advantage | Why It Matters |
|----------------|----------------|
| **Legally clean** | No one can accuse commission of favoritism |
| **Simple** | No model to explain, no weights to justify |
| **Predictable** | Farmer knows: submit early = get funded |
| **Budget reality** | Money arrives in tranches — you process what's in front of you |

FIFO is a **rational default** when you have no scoring system and need to minimize corruption risk.

### Where FIFO Fails (Our Opportunity)

FIFO breaks down specifically at **the budget allocation decision point**:

```
Budget available: 5B tenge
Queue of approved applications: 2,854
Total requested: 12B tenge
You can only fund ~1,200

FIFO says: Fund the first 1,200 who submitted
WE say:   Fund the 1,200 that maximize impact
```

**This is the ONLY decision point we're changing.** We're not replacing the inspector's review, not changing the eligibility criteria, not touching the automatic GISS checks.

---

## How Each Solution Maps to the Real System

### Solution 1: Impact Score
**Where it fits:** AFTER inspector approves, BEFORE budget allocation

```
Current:
  Inspector approves → Queue (FIFO) → Budget available → Pay in order

Proposed:
  Inspector approves → Queue (ranked by Impact Score) → Budget available → Pay by rank
```

**Why this respects the system:**
- Inspector still makes approve/reject decision (we don't override)
- Automatic GISS checks still happen (ISJ, IBSPR, KGD)
- We only change the **queue ordering** for the "waiting for budget" pool
- Farmer still submits the same way through subsidy.plem.kz

**Budget awareness:** The score should NOT penalize late-year applications. A farmer who submits in August isn't worse — their budget region may just be depleted. Our score focuses on **impact**, not timing.

### Solution 2: Fraud Risk
**Where it fits:** PARALLEL to inspector review, as a decision support tool

```
Current:
  Inspector reviews manually → catches some issues, misses patterns

Proposed:  
  Inspector reviews + sees fraud risk flags → focuses manual review on high-risk
```

**Why this respects the system:**
- Doesn't auto-reject anything
- Flags for human review (the task brief says: "AI assists, not replaces")
- Addresses the ВАП finding (66B tenge wasted)

### Solution 3: Dashboard
**Where it fits:** For commission/analyst oversight, not individual decisions

Shows patterns invisible in the current system:
- Akmola has 50.7% backlog but Pavlodar has 2.1% — should budget be redistributed?
- Tyulkubas: 86% to one recipient — is this fair?
- Simpson's Paradox: the same policy produces opposite effects in different subsidy types

---

## Revised Solution Framing

Instead of "replace FIFO with scoring," the pitch should be:

> **"Keep FIFO for the application flow. Add scoring for the budget allocation decision. Add fraud flags for inspector support. Add a dashboard for commission oversight."**

This is more honest and more realistic. The commission keeps control.

---

## How This Changes the Build Plan

| Phase | What | Accounts for system reality |
|-------|------|----------------------------|
| **1. ML** | Train on "approved waiting for budget" pool (2,854) vs "already funded" (28,627) | Scores the RIGHT population — those in the queue |
| **2. Fraud** | Isolation Forest on ALL 36K records | Parallel to main process, doesn't interfere |
| **3. Dashboard** | Show budget utilization by oblast, queue depth, monopolization | Helps commission allocate budgets between regions |
| **4. Simulator** | "If we had 5B tenge, how would FIFO vs Merit distribute?" | The killer demo slide |

### Planner Response

No — the opposite. Let me clarify:

**We ARE replacing FIFO with merit-based scoring.** But only at one specific point:

```
THE DECISION POINT:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  2,854 approved applications waiting          │
│  Budget arrives: enough for ~1,200            │
│                                               │
│  FIFO:  Fund #1-1200 by submission date       │
│  US:    Fund top 1200 by Impact Score    ← THIS│
└──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**What I'm NOT saying:**
- ❌ Go back to FIFO
- ❌ Keep the current system as-is

**What I AM saying:**
- ✅ Replace FIFO with merit-based scoring for budget allocation
- ✅ But respect everything else the system does (inspector review, automatic checks)
- ✅ Frame it as "improving the queue ordering" not "replacing the entire system"

This matters for the **presentation**. If you say "we replace your system," the ministry pushes back. If you say "we improve the one decision you struggle with most — who gets funded when budget is limited," they listen.

The technical implementation is the same either way:
1. Train XGBoost on all 36K records
2. Score every application
3. Rank by score instead of submission date
4. Show SHAP explanations for each score

The difference is in **framing** — we're building the same thing, just positioning it smarter.

# Complete Inventory of Ideas (from all previous sessions)

## Ideas We Haven't Discussed Yet

### 1. Uplift Modeling — "Where will the subsidy have the MOST effect?"
From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/wow-factor-strategy-4e60cc.md:19-37`

This is the **strongest idea** we haven't talked about. Instead of "who is the best farmer?", ask "who will CHANGE THE MOST with a subsidy?"

- Farmer A: score 90, but with/without subsidy → 2% difference (he's fine anyway)
- Farmer B: score 60, but with subsidy → 40% growth (subsidy is the catalyst)

Normal scoring funds Farmer A. Uplift modeling funds Farmer B. **This is what the government actually needs.**

Problem: We don't have outcome data (what happened AFTER subsidy was given). We can proxy it, but it's honest to call it estimated.

### 2. Counterfactual Explanations — "What should the farmer change?"
Instead of "your score is 45, goodbye" → "If you had 15% more cattle OR had a processing facility, your score would be 72."

This turns rejection into a **development roadmap** for the farmer.

### 3. Portfolio Theory (Markowitz for Subsidies)
From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/wow-factor-strategy-4e60cc.md:168-180`

Treat subsidy allocation like investment portfolio optimization:
- Each farmer = "asset" with expected ROI and risk
- Optimal set of recipients = efficient frontier
- Commission picks a point: "max growth with moderate risk" or "guaranteed minimum result"

**inDrive is a fintech company** — jury will recognize this framing.

### 4. Benford's Law Anomaly Detection
From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/verified-patterns-4e60cc.md:40-54`

Shymkent deviates from Benford's Law: digit "1" appears 15.8% vs expected 30.1%. Explained by uniform milk subsidies (not fraud), but it's a **killer visualization** and shows analytical depth.

### 5. Rapid-Fire Farmer Identification
From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/verified-patterns-4e60cc.md:64-73`

93 pairs of applications submitted <30 seconds apart from same district = same farmer submitting multiple subsidy types. These multi-type farmers have 3.9% reject rate vs 9.2% single-type. **More organized farmers succeed more.**

### 6. Phased Allocation (CDC Vaccine Model)
From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/extended-fairness-research-4e60cc.md:154-158`

Instead of one ranking → 4 phases:
- Phase 1: Critical (budget deficit >50%, disaster zones)
- Phase 2: High priority (breeding, processing)
- Phase 3: Standard
- Phase 4: All remaining

### 7. Fairness Constraints
- **Leximin Minimum Quota**: Every district gets ≥2% budget (prevents total exclusion)
- **Creamy Layer Exclusion** (India model): Exclude large agro-holdings from priority categories
- **Counterfactual Fairness Check**: "Would this score change if the farmer were from a different district?"

### 8. LLM-Powered Explanations
Natural language generation for scores: "Your application scored 72. The main factors were: small farm in an underserved region (+15), breeding cattle acquisition (+10), but high district concentration (-8)."

Could use GPT-4o-mini ($7.13 for all 36K records) or Cloudflare AI Workers (free tier).

### 9. Pre-Check System
From the data: 65% of rejections are retries. If we show farmers likely issues BEFORE submission, we reduce friction massively. Client-side rule checker based on Appendix 2 of the regulations.

## Proposed Solution Stack (Simple → Scale)

### Layer 1: ML Pipeline (MUST — this is the engine)
1. **XGBoost model** trained on real data (rejection prediction → scoring)
2. **SHAP explanations** per record
3. **Isolation Forest** anomaly scores
4. **Rule-based fraud flags** (monopoly district, rapid-fire, Benford deviation)
5. Export everything to JSON

### Layer 2: Budget Simulator (MUST — this is the WOW)
- Slider: budget amount
- Toggle: FIFO vs Merit vs Equity-focused
- Show: who gets funded, Gini coefficient, small farmer %, regional distribution
- **This single feature elevates us from "scoring tool" to "policy decision platform"**

### Layer 3: Explanations (MUST — this is the trust)
- **SHAP waterfall** per record (for commission)
- **Counterfactual** per record: "change X to improve score by Y" (for farmer)
- **Fairness audit**: regional bias check (for auditors)

### Layer 4: Dashboard (MUST — this is the UX)
- All 36K records with pagination
- Filters: oblast, district, subsidy type, score range
- Fraud flags highlighted
- Benford's Law visualization (small but impressive)

### Layer 5: Stretch Goals (NICE — if time allows)
- Uplift estimation (proxy-based)
- LLM natural language explanations
- Portfolio optimization visualization
- Pre-Check form

## What Makes This "Genius" vs "Good"

| Other Teams | Us |
|-------------|------|
| Score farmers | Score + explain + show counterfactuals |
| Show dashboard | Show **policy simulator** |
| Predict rejection | Rank by **impact** (honest about ceiling) |
| Ignore fairness | **Quantify** fairness (Gini, Leximin) |
| No fraud detection | Isolation Forest + Benford's Law |
| Mock data or 500 rows | All 36,651 real records |

The key differentiator: we're not building a scoring tool. We're building a **subsidy allocation decision platform** with 3 users: the **commission** (simulator + rankings), the **farmer** (explanations + roadmap), and the **auditor** (fraud flags + fairness metrics).

## Audit: Why Each Idea Was Never Built

I traced the full history across your previous sessions. Here's the honest post-mortem:

| Session | What Happened | Output |

| `4e60cc` (Research) | 4+ brainstorming sessions: fairness theory, patterns, wow strategy, data analysis | **3,600+ lines of docs, 0 lines of code** |
| `5d7c25` (Planning) | Lean MVP plan, design system, architecture | Plans with 22-29 hour estimates |

### Why Each Idea Died

#### 1. Uplift Modeling (the #1 "genius" idea)
**Why planned:** It's the strongest differentiation. "Where will the subsidy have the MOST effect?"
**Why never built:** 
- **No outcome data.** Our Excel has applications and statuses, but NOT what happened after subsidy was received (production change, revenue growth). Without treatment outcomes, uplift is impossible to train.
- The mitigation was "semi-synthetic approach" = fabricate outcomes. This is dishonest and the model would learn nothing real.
- **Verdict:** Correctly abandoned. We can't do real uplift. But we CAN estimate a **proxy** (e.g., "subsidy as % of estimated farm value" or "district with lowest current coverage = highest marginal impact"). This is weaker but honest.

#### 2. Portfolio Theory (Markowitz)
**Why planned:** Elegant framing for inDrive (fintech) jury.
**Why never built:**
- Requires ROI estimates per farmer. We have zero economic outcome data.
- Easy to visualize, impossible to populate with real numbers.
- **Verdict:** Could be a **visualization concept** in the simulator (efficient frontier curve), but the underlying data would be estimated. Good for presentation, bad for scientific rigor.

#### 3. Counterfactual Explanations
**Why planned:** Turns rejection into a development roadmap for farmers.
**Why never built:**
- Requires a trained model first (to perturb inputs and observe score changes). The model was never trained — it was always heuristic.
- Heuristic counterfactuals are trivial but meaningless ("change your district" isn't actionable).
- **Verdict:** Becomes trivially easy once we have XGBoost + SHAP. Should be built. It's just: perturb top-3 SHAP features → show new score.

#### 4. Budget Simulator
**Why planned:** Marked "KEEP" in lean MVP, described as the WOW feature.
**Why never built (properly):**
- [simulate.ts](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/agroscore/src/pages/api/simulate.ts:0:0-0:0) existed but the data layer feeding it was broken (empty arrays).
- The frontend fetched from a hardcoded 500-row JSON file instead of all 36K.
- **Root cause:** Built the UI before the data pipeline. Classic frontend-first mistake.
- **Verdict:** MUST build. Simple to implement once the data pipeline works. It's literally: sort by timestamp (FIFO) vs sort by score (Merit), pick top N that fit budget, compare metrics.

#### 5. Fraud Detection (Isolation Forest)
**Why planned:** 66B tenge wasted (Audit Chamber finding). Anti-corruption resonates with government jury.
**Why never built:**
- [ml/score.py](file:///Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/ml/score.py:0:0-0:0) used heuristic fraud_risk (rule-based), never trained Isolation Forest.
- **No ground truth for fraud.** We don't know which applications were actually fraudulent.
- The AI-INTEGRATION.md warned about this: "Cluster ≠ fraud ring — it's a hypothesis for audit."
- **Verdict:** Still worth building. Isolation Forest doesn't need labels — it finds statistical outliers. The key is honest framing: "anomaly flag for human review," not "fraud detection."

#### 6. LLM Explanations
**Why planned:** Makes technical ML accessible to non-technical commission members.
**Why never built:**
- Requires API key + costs money ($7 for all 36K records with GPT-4o-mini).
- Adds external dependency and latency.
- Not core to scoring.
- **Verdict:** Stretch goal. Can be added in 2 hours IF the core pipeline works. Low priority.

#### 7. Pre-Check System
**Why planned:** 65% of rejections are retries. Reducing friction is genuinely valuable.
**Why never built (properly):**
- Real eligibility criteria (BIN registration, ISJ animal registry, land ownership) aren't in our dataset.
- We can only check what we can see: district, subsidy type, amount, timing.
- The PreCheckForm existed but used invented rules.
- **Verdict:** Can build a simplified version: "Applications from your district + type have X% approval rate. Historical issues: [list]." Honest and useful, not pretending to know more than we do.

#### 8. Fairness Constraints (Leximin, EF1, Counterfactual Fairness)
**Why planned:** 3,600 lines of fairness research across 11 "waves."
**Why never built:**
- Academic concepts that are hard to operationalize.
- Required a working model first (to test for bias), which never existed.
- **The research was the product, not the code.** 11 waves of research with zero implementation.
- **Verdict:** Pick ONE. Leximin minimum quota (every district ≥2% budget) is simple and powerful. Add to simulator as a constraint toggle.

#### 9. Steppe Futurism Design
**Why planned:** "Something no other team will have." Three.js, GSAP, particle systems.
**Why never built:**
- 233-line design spec requiring Three.js, GSAP ScrollTrigger, WebGL, custom Canvas, tsParticles.
- Estimated 20+ hours for one frontend developer.
- Pure scope fantasy.
- **Verdict:** Dead. Use clean Tailwind + a good color palette. Focus on data, not particles.

---

## Root Cause Analysis

The pattern across ALL previous sessions:

```
Research (hours) ──→ Planning (hours) ──→ More Research ──→ Revised Plan
       ↓                    ↓                    ↓               ↓
  3,600 lines           5 plan files        More docs       Even more docs
  of docs               309-line MVP        2 design        17 recommended
                        plan                systems         features
                                                                ↓
                                                        Implementation
                                                        starts late
                                                                ↓
                                                        Broken data layer
                                                        Heuristic scoring
                                                        500 rows instead of 36K
                                                        No ML model trained
```

**5 root causes:**

1. **Research addiction** — Each brainstorming session spawned 3 new ideas instead of converging. 11 "waves" of fairness research produced 3,600 lines but zero code.

2. **No hard stop between research and implementation** — The plan kept expanding instead of freezing scope.

3. **Frontend before backend** — Built UI components that consumed from a broken data layer. The data pipeline (THE FOUNDATION) was never properly completed.

4. **Complexity ratchet** — Started with Cloudflare Workers + D1 + SSR + Solid.js + GSAP + Three.js when a static page + JSON + XGBoost would have been enough.

5. **Solo execution with team-sized plans** — Plans reference DEV-1, DEV-2, DEV-3, Domain Expert. Actual team appears to be one person + AI.

---

## Final Conclusion

### What to ACTUALLY build (hard scope freeze)

**The only question that matters: what can ONE person build in the remaining time that demonstrates real ML value?**

| Priority | Feature | Why | Time |
|----------|---------|-----|------|
| **P0** | `pipeline.py`: Excel → Clean → Features → XGBoost → SHAP → JSON | This is the CORE. Without this, nothing else matters. | 2-3h |
| **P0** | Budget Simulator (FIFO vs Merit comparison) | Single most impressive demo feature. Dead simple once scores exist. | 1h |
| **P1** | Isolation Forest anomaly scores + fraud flags | Easy, no labels needed, impressive | 30min |
| **P1** | Counterfactual explanations (perturb top SHAP features) | Trivial once model exists | 30min |
| **P1** | Dashboard: all 36K records, filters, score breakdown | The visual layer | 2-3h |
| **P2** | Leximin fairness constraint in simulator | One toggle, massive intellectual depth | 30min |
| **P2** | Benford's Law visualization | 20 lines of code, impressive on screen | 20min |
| **NEVER** | Three.js, GSAP, particle systems, scrollytelling | Scope fantasy | — |
| **NEVER** | Real uplift modeling (no outcome data) | Intellectually dishonest without data | — |
| **NEVER** | MCP server, LLM chat assistant | Buzzword padding | — |

### The Pitch (5 minutes)

1. **"66B tenge distributed by FIFO"** — hook
2. **Simpson's Paradox** — "we found you can't use one model for all subsidy types" — credibility
3. **Case-level dedup: 9.2% → 3.7%** — "the real rejection rate is much lower than it looks" — insight
4. **Live demo: Budget Simulator** — "watch what happens when we switch from FIFO to merit" — WOW
5. **SHAP explanation** — click one farmer, show why their score is what it is — trust
6. **Fraud flag** — "we found anomalies the current system misses" — anti-corruption
7. **Honest limitations** — "69% of rejection variance is invisible to us" — respect

### The Anti-Pattern to Avoid

**NO MORE RESEARCH. NO MORE PLANNING. BUILD.**

The docs are good enough. The features are identified. The data is understood. The only thing missing is **working code.**

### Planner Response

## LLM: Where It Helps vs Where It's Wasted

### For Anti-Fraud: LLM is the Wrong Tool

Anti-fraud detection is fundamentally a **structured data problem**:

```
Input:  36K rows × 15 numerical/categorical features
Task:   Find statistical outliers
Output: anomaly_score per record
```

| Approach | What it does | Accuracy | Speed | Cost |
|----------|-------------|----------|-------|------|
| **Isolation Forest** | Mathematically isolates outliers in feature space | High (designed for this) | 2 seconds for 36K rows | Free |
| **LLM** | "Look at this row, does it seem fraudulent?" | Unreliable (hallucination risk) | 36K API calls | $7+ |

**LLMs can't do math reliably.** Asking GPT "is this farmer's volume 3σ above the district median?" is asking it to do something Isolation Forest does natively in milliseconds.

**For fraud, the correct architecture is:**

```
Isolation Forest (DETECT) → SHAP (EXPLAIN why flagged) → Dashboard (SHOW to auditor)
```

No LLM needed in this chain. The auditor sees: "This application was flagged because: volume is 4.2x district median, district has 86% monopoly concentration, submission was 12 seconds after another from same district."

That's already explainable without LLM.

---

### Where LLM Actually Adds Value

LLMs are good at **one thing we can't do with traditional ML**: converting structured data into natural language for non-technical users.

| Use Case | Without LLM | With LLM |
|----------|-------------|----------|
| **Score explanation** | "SHAP: district_reject_rate=+0.15, is_small_farmer=-0.08" | "Ваша заявка получила 68 баллов. Основная причина — высокий процент отказов в вашем районе (30.8%)." |
| **Fraud flag** | "anomaly_score=0.87, flags: monopoly_district, volume_outlier" | "Внимание: эта заявка помечена для проверки. Объём производства в 4.2 раза превышает средний по району." |
| **Shortlist summary** | JSON with 100 top-ranked records | "Рекомендуем к финансированию 100 заявок общей суммой 2.3 млрд тг. 62% — мелкие фермеры, 14 областей представлены." |

**But these are all PRESENTATION enhancements, not core AI.** The scoring, ranking, and fraud detection work without LLM. LLM just makes the output prettier.

---

### My Honest Recommendation

```
CORE AI (must build, does the actual work):
├── XGBoost         → Scoring & ranking
├── SHAP            → Explainability (per-record)
├── Isolation Forest → Anomaly detection
└── Counterfactual  → "What to change" recommendations

PRESENTATION LAYER (nice to have, makes output human-readable):
└── LLM (GPT-4o-mini) → Natural language summaries
    ├── Pre-compute for all 36K records (batch, not real-time)
    ├── Template: "Given these SHAP values, explain in Russian"
    └── Cost: ~$7, Time: ~30 min batch processing
```

### When to Add LLM

**After** the core pipeline works and produces real scores + SHAP + anomaly flags. Then:

1. Take top-500 records (or all 36K)
2. Batch-process through GPT-4o-mini with a template:
   ```
   "Given: farmer in {oblast}, {subsidy_type}, score {score}/100.
    Top factors: {shap_top_3}.
    Generate a 2-sentence explanation in Russian."
   ```
3. Store in JSON alongside the scores
4. Display in dashboard as a human-readable explanation card

This is ~1-2 hours of work, but ONLY after the core ML is working.

### The Anti-Pattern to Avoid

Don't use LLM as a substitute for actual ML:
- ❌ "GPT, analyze this dataset for fraud" — it can't
- ❌ "GPT, score this farmer" — it'll hallucinate a number
- ❌ "GPT, find patterns in these 36K records" — it has a context window limit

Use LLM as a **translation layer**: ML produces structured output → LLM translates to human language.

---

### Bottom Line for the ТЗ

The ТЗ says:
> *"analyze it using AI/ML (not just filtering or keyword search)"*
> *"return a result with a brief explanation"*

**XGBoost + SHAP satisfies both requirements.** LLM is bonus for UX/explainability points, not a requirement.

Shall we now write the master architecture doc and implementation plan? I want to make sure we nail the core design before coding.

### Planner Response

## The Best Subsidy Scoring System: First Principles

Let me reason from the ground up, ignoring time.

### The Fundamental Question: What Is "Merit"?

The ТЗ says: *"move to merit-based scoring."* But merit has **multiple valid definitions**:

| Definition | What it optimizes | Example |
|-----------|-------------------|---------|
| **Efficiency** | Max output per tenge | Fund the farmer who'll produce the most milk per subsidy tenge |
| **Impact (Uplift)** | Max CHANGE from subsidy | Fund the farmer whose life changes most WITH vs WITHOUT subsidy |
| **Fairness** | Min inequality | Fund small farmers in underserved regions first |
| **Strategic** | National goals | Fund breeding/food security priorities per Concept 2021-2030 |
| **Risk** | Min waste/fraud | Avoid funding applications with anomalous patterns |

**A single XGBoost model picking ONE definition = mediocre.** Every other hackathon team will do this.

**The real insight: The commission should CHOOSE how to weight these dimensions.** Different political priorities = different weight configurations = different shortlists. This is what a real ministry needs.

---

### The Architecture That Matters

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              DIMENSION ENGINES (independent)              │
├──────────┬──────────┬──────────┬──────────┬─────────────┤
│  Risk    │ Fairness │Strategic │Efficiency│ Fraud Risk  │
│  Score   │  Score   │  Score   │  Score   │   Score     │
│          │          │          │          │             │
│ XGBoost  │ Computed │ Rules +  │ Computed │ Isolation   │
│ per type │ from data│ policy   │ proxy    │ Forest +    │
│ + SHAP   │          │ weights  │          │ rules       │
├──────────┴──────────┴──────────┴──────────┴─────────────┤
│                                                           │
│         CONFIGURABLE WEIGHT VECTOR                        │
│         [Commission adjusts via sliders]                  │
│         Default: 25% / 20% / 20% / 20% / 15%            │
│                                                           │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                           │
│         COMPOSITE IMPACT SCORE [0-100]                    │
│         + SHAP explanation per record                     │
│         + Counterfactual "what to change"                 │
│                                                           │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                           │
│         BUDGET ALLOCATOR                                  │
│         Budget input → Ranked shortlist                   │
│         Compare: FIFO vs Merit vs Custom weights          │
│         Metrics: Gini, small farmer %, regional spread    │
│                                                           │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**This is not one model. It's a FRAMEWORK.** And that's what makes it production-viable — the ministry can adjust priorities without retraining models.

---

### Each Dimension: What ML Does and Doesn't Do

#### 1. Risk Score (XGBoost — real ML)
- **What**: Train to predict rejection probability
- **How**: Ensemble of 3-4 models per subsidy type group (addresses Simpson's Paradox)
- **Output**: P(rejection) per record + SHAP values
- **Why not one model**: Simpson's Paradox. Code 01300 has 0.8% reject, code 03900 has 36.1%. One model averages these out and learns nothing.
- **Honest limitation**: 31% of variance explained. We show this openly.
- **Value**: Not "this WILL be rejected" but "applications with this profile tend to face higher scrutiny"

#### 2. Fairness Score (Computed — deterministic)
- **Components**:
  - `is_small_farmer`: binary from herd size / sum analysis
  - `underserved_region`: district with low historical funding per capita
  - `concentration_penalty`: negative for districts with monopoly (top-1 share >50%)
  - `first_time_applicant`: proxy from case-level dedup (no prior successful application)
- **Not ML**: These are direct computations from data. No model needed.
- **Why it matters**: Without this, scoring would favor large, well-connected farms. The ТЗ mentions "according to actual need or merit."

#### 3. Strategic Alignment Score (Rules + Policy)
- **Components**:
  - Food security priority: poultry (58% self-sufficiency = high priority), dairy, breeding
  - National Concept 2021-2030 alignment
  - Regional specialization index (is this farm in a region suited for this activity?)
- **Not ML**: Policy-driven rules. Ministry defines what's strategic.
- **Why it matters**: Subsidies exist to achieve national goals, not just reward "good" farmers.

#### 4. Efficiency Score (Computed proxy)
- **Components**:
  - `relative_volume`: production volume vs type+oblast median
  - `sum_efficiency`: amount requested relative to expected output
  - `acquisition_vs_production`: acquisition subsidies = higher risk, production = ongoing
- **Limitation**: We don't have actual farm output data. This is estimated from subsidy amounts and norms.
- **Why it matters**: Two farmers requesting the same amount may produce very different outputs.

#### 5. Fraud Risk Score (Isolation Forest — real ML)
- **Components**:
  - Isolation Forest anomaly score on [volume, amount, district_concentration, retry_velocity]
  - Rule-based flags: monopoly_district, rapid_fire, benford_deviation, volume_outlier
- **Output**: fraud_risk ∈ [0, 1] + list of triggered flags
- **Honest limitation**: "Anomaly ≠ fraud. This is a signal for human review."

---

### Why This Is Better Than Alternatives

| Approach | Problem |
|----------|---------|
| **Only XGBoost + SHAP** | Predicts rejection (boring, every team does it). Doesn't address "what is merit?" |
| **Only heuristic scoring** | No ML. Judges will see it's just weighted averages. |
| **All 17 ideas from research** | Nothing works. Half-finished features everywhere. |
| **Multi-dimensional framework** | Each dimension is independently testable. Configurable. Honest about what it can/can't do. |

### The Budget Simulator: Why It Elevates Everything

Without a simulator, we're saying "this farmer scored 78." So what?

With a simulator: *"If you allocate 50B tenge using FIFO, 12 districts get nothing and Gini is 0.68. Using our merit-based scoring with fairness weight=30%, all districts get funding and Gini drops to 0.52. Small farmer share increases from 24% to 34%."*

**This is the single most impressive demo feature.** It turns abstract scores into visible policy outcomes. The commission can SEE what happens when they change priorities.

---

### What NOT to Build (and why)

| Idea | Why Skip |
|------|----------|
| **Uplift Modeling** | No outcome data. We'd be fabricating treatment effects. Dishonest. |
| **Portfolio Theory** | Needs ROI per farmer. Can't estimate without production outcome data. |
| **Quadratic Funding** | Wrong mechanism — no voting in subsidy process. |
| **Deferred Acceptance** | Wrong context — farmers don't rank subsidy types. |
| **Full Pre-Check** | Real eligibility criteria (BIN, ISJ, land) aren't in our data. |
| **MCP Server** | Buzzword. No real user would connect Claude to this. |
| **Three.js/Particles** | Scope fantasy from Steppe Futurism plan. |

| Component | Type | ML? | Build Time | Impact |
|-----------|------|-----|-----------|--------|
| **XGBoost Ensemble** (3-4 models per subsidy group) | Supervised | ✅ | 2h | Core |
| **SHAP per record** | Explainability | ✅ | Built into XGBoost | Core |

## Dimension-by-Dimension Audit

### ❌ Efficiency Score — BROKEN, Should Be Dropped

From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/verified-patterns-4e60cc.md:56-62`:

> "98.1% сумм — ТОЧНЫЕ КРАТНЫЕ нормативу. sum = norm × volume for 98.1% of cases."

This means **every applicant within the same subsidy type has identical per-unit cost** (it's the norm, set by regulation). There's no efficiency variation to measure. `sum/norm = volume = herd_size`. The "Efficiency Score" would just re-measure farm size, which is already in the Fairness dimension.

**Verdict:** This dimension is fake. Drop it.

---

### ⚠️ Risk Score (XGBoost) — Needs Deconfounding

The model predicts rejection. But rejection has **multiple causes we can't distinguish**:

| Cause | % of rejections (est.) | Visible in data? |
|-------|----------------------|-----------------|
| Budget exhausted (FIFO timing) | ~40% | ⚠️ Only via temporal patterns |
| Missing documents | ~30% | ❌ Not visible |
| Actual ineligibility | ~15% | ❌ Not visible |
| Inspector discretion | ~15% | ❌ Not visible |

From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/verified-patterns-4e60cc.md:75-93`:

```
Month | Reject Rate
  2   |  13.7%  ← Budget exhaustion wave 1
  4   |   4.4%  ← Minimum (new budget tranche)
  8   |  13.3%  ← Budget exhaustion wave 2
```

If we naively train XGBoost, it learns **"applying in August = bad"** — which is just FIFO timing, not application quality. The model would encode the exact problem we're trying to solve (FIFO bias).

**What needs to happen:**
1. Include `budget_pressure_month` (cumulative spending proxy) as a **control variable** — the model sees it, accounts for it, but the SHAP contribution of timing is separated from application quality
2. OR: Train only on April-May applications (4.4% rejection = mostly real eligibility issues) as a "clean" training set
3. OR: Use inverse propensity weighting — upweight rejections from low-pressure months, downweight from high-pressure months

This is significantly more sophisticated than "just train XGBoost."

---

### ⚠️ Fairness Score — Too Binary

Current proposal: `is_small_farmer` (binary), `underserved_region` (binary), `concentration_penalty` (threshold).

**Problem:** Reality is continuous. A farm with 10 cattle and 100 cattle are both "small" vs 10,000, but they're completely different situations. Binary flags erase this.

**What it should be:**

```
fairness_index = (
    farm_size_equity          # Continuous: percentile rank within type (smaller = higher)
    × regional_equity         # Continuous: district funding/capita vs national average
    × access_equity           # Continuous: inverse of retry_count (new applicants get boost)
    × concentration_correction # Continuous: inverse HHI in district
)
```

Plus **interaction effects**: a small farmer in an underserved region should score MULTIPLICATIVELY higher, not just additively. A large farmer in a concentrated district should be penalized multiplicatively.

```python
# Bad (additive binary):
fairness = 0.4 * is_small + 0.3 * is_underserved + 0.3 * is_first_time

# Better (continuous multiplicative):
fairness = (
    farm_size_percentile_score    # 0-100, smaller = higher
    * (1 + regional_deficit_ratio) # multiplier: underserved = 1.5x, avg = 1.0x
    * (1 + access_novelty_bonus)   # multiplier: first-time = 1.3x
    * concentration_correction     # 0-1: monopoly district = 0.5x
)
```

---

### ⚠️ Strategic Score — Too Vague

"Food security priority" is hand-wavy. The Concept 2021-2030 has **specific numeric targets**. Strategic alignment should be computed as:

```
strategic_score = sector_gap × regional_fit
```

Where:
- **sector_gap** = (target_production − current_production) / target_production for the subsidy's sector. Poultry has 42% gap (58% self-sufficiency) → high priority. Beef might be at 95% → low priority.
- **regional_fit** = specialization index from `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/verified-patterns-4e60cc.md:95-108`. Camel breeding in Mangystau (9.1x index) = perfectly suited. Camel breeding in Almaty city = misaligned.

This is a real calculation, not a hand-waved rule.

---

### ⚠️ Fraud/Anomaly Score — Needs Layering

Isolation Forest treats all features equally. But a farm that's 3σ above volume median might just be a large farm. A farm that submits 12 seconds after another from the same district is actually suspicious.

**Should be layered:**

| Layer | What it detects | Method | Severity |
|-------|----------------|--------|----------|
| **Statistical** | Volume/amount outliers | Isolation Forest on numeric features | Low (could be legitimate) |
| **Behavioral** | Rapid-fire, weekend timing, retry velocity | Rule-based + temporal analysis | Medium |
| **Distributional** | Benford's deviation, non-multiple of norm | Statistical tests per district | Medium |
| **Structural** | Monopoly district, single-producer dominance | HHI + concentration analysis | High |

Each layer produces a sub-score. Combined with **severity-weighted aggregation**, not simple average.

---

## Revised Architecture: 4 Dimensions, Not 5

Drop Efficiency (it's fake). Merge Strategic + Economic into "Impact Potential."

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    4 DIMENSION ENGINES                       │
├──────────────┬──────────────┬───────────────┬──────────────┤
│  Approval    │   Impact     │   Fairness    │   Anomaly    │
│  Likelihood  │   Potential  │    Index      │    Score     │
│              │              │               │              │
│ XGBoost      │ sector_gap   │ farm_size     │ Isolation    │
│ per type     │ × regional   │ × regional    │ Forest       │
│ deconfounded │   _fit       │   _equity     │ + behavioral │
│ for budget   │ × multiplier │ × access      │ + distribut. │
│ pressure     │   _effect    │   _equity     │ + structural │
│              │              │ × concentr.   │              │
│ + SHAP       │              │   _correction │              │
├──────────────┴──────────────┴───────────────┴──────────────┤
│                                                              │
│  COMPOSITE = w1×Approval + w2×Impact + w3×Fairness - w4×Anom│
│  [Commission controls w1..w4 via sliders]                    │
│  Default: 30% / 25% / 30% / 15%                             │
│                                                              │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  BUDGET ALLOCATOR: Score → Rank → Top-N within budget        │
│  + Leximin constraint: every district gets ≥ min allocation  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Why 4, Not 5

| # | Dimension | What it answers | ML type |
|---|-----------|----------------|---------|
| 1 | **Approval Likelihood** | "Will this pass the current system?" | Supervised ML (XGBoost) |
| 2 | **Impact Potential** | "How much value does this create?" | Computed (policy + data) |
| 3 | **Fairness Index** | "How much does this advance equity?" | Computed (continuous, multiplicative) |
| 4 | **Anomaly Score** | "Should this be flagged for review?" | Unsupervised ML (Isolation Forest) + rules |

Each one:
- Has clear sub-components that are independently verifiable
- Is more sophisticated than "a weighted average of binary flags"
- Uses the appropriate tool (ML where ML helps, computation where it's deterministic)
- Is honest about limitations

### The Key Insight for Judges

We're not showing 5 simple scores. We're showing **4 deeply-reasoned dimensions**, each with:
- Sub-components justified by data analysis
- Methodological choices we can defend (deconfounding, multiplicative fairness, layered anomaly detection)
- Known limitations we openly state

This is the difference between "we built a scoring system" and "we thought deeply about what scoring MEANS for subsidies."
