## 1. Which Merit Formula Is Best for Combining Dimensions?

The research explicitly studied this at `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/extended-fairness-research-4e60cc.md:307-318` (MCDA section) and arrived at a critical finding at lines 891-901:

> **Arrow's Impossibility Theorem: It's impossible to create a "perfect" ranking system. Any system has tradeoffs. You must explicitly choose which properties to prioritize.**

So there's no "best" formula. But the research did identify which real-world systems work BEST for our context:

| System | How it combines | Why it works | Limitation |
|--------|----------------|-------------|-----------|
| **NIH Grants** | Independent scored criteria → Overall Impact → Percentile | Multiple experts score independently, aggregate. Transparent. | Needs multiple reviewers (we have ML instead) |
| **Australia Immigration** | Points per criterion, configurable threshold | Simple, transparent, adjustable by policy. Citizens understand it. | Weights are politically determined |
| **MELD Score (Organ transplant)** | Single formula: `9.57 × ln(creatinine) + 3.78 × ln(bilirubin) + ...` | Objective, no human bias, validated on outcomes | Needs outcome data for calibration |
| **CDC Vaccine** | Ethical principles → phased allocation → within-phase scoring | Values-explicit, phased reduces pressure on scoring accuracy | Requires pre-defined phases |

**The convergence**: The best systems share 3 properties:
1. **Dimensions are scored independently** (not mixed into one opaque model)
2. **Combination method is transparent and adjustable** (not fixed weights)
3. **Result is shown as percentile within category** (not absolute score)

**For us**: Weighted sum with configurable sliders IS the right combination method — not because it's mathematically optimal, but because:
- Commission members can understand it
- Weights can be adjusted without retraining models
- Each dimension is independently auditable
- It satisfies the ТЗ explainability requirement

BUT — the **dimension engines themselves** need to be more rigorous than simple binary flags. That's where the real research applies.

---

## 2. How to Measure Fairness Properly

The research found that **fairness is not ONE metric. It's a SYSTEM of 4 layers**:

### Layer A: Floor Guarantee (Leximin)
From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/extended-fairness-research-4e60cc.md:33` and line 115-119:

> **Leximin (Rawls)**: Maximize the minimum, then the second minimum.

```
Step 1: Every district gets ≥ min_quota of budget (e.g., 2%)
Step 2: Remaining budget allocated by merit score
```

This prevents **total exclusion** — no district gets zero funding. The Rawls' Difference Principle (line 678-688) backs this: *"Inequality is OK only if it benefits the least advantaged."*

This is NOT part of the scoring formula. It's a **constraint on the allocation step**.

### Layer B: Progressive Weighting (Continuous, Not Binary)
From Brazil PRONAF (line 14, 105-107) and Tiered Pricing (lines 581-591):

```python
# BAD: Binary
fairness_size = 1.0 if is_small_farmer else 0.0

# GOOD: Continuous progressive (Brazil PRONAF model)
# Smaller farms get progressively higher fairness score
# Using log-scale: a farm at 10th percentile by size gets ~90 fairness
# A farm at 90th percentile gets ~10 fairness
farm_size_percentile = percentile_rank(volume, within=subsidy_type)
fairness_size = 100 * (1 - farm_size_percentile)  # Inverted: smaller = higher
```

From India's Creamy Layer (lines 334-343):

```python
# Exclusion: Large agro-holdings removed from priority categories entirely
if farm_size_percentile > 0.95:  # Top 5% = "creamy layer"
    fairness_size = 0  # Excluded from fairness bonus
```

### Layer C: Regional Equity (FEMA Vulnerability Index model)
From FEMA (lines 839-848):

```python
# Regional equity = how underserved is this district?
district_funding_per_farmer = total_district_funding / district_farmer_count
national_avg = total_national_funding / national_farmer_count
regional_equity = max(0, 1 - (district_funding_per_farmer / national_avg))
# 0 = well-funded district, 1 = severely underfunded
```

### Layer D: Post-Hoc Audit (Counterfactual Fairness)
From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/extended-fairness-research-4e60cc.md:131-141`:

```python
# AFTER scoring: check if district/oblast causes bias
original_score = model.predict(application)
for other_district in all_districts:
    counterfactual = application.copy()
    counterfactual['district'] = other_district
    cf_score = model.predict(counterfactual)
    if abs(original_score - cf_score) > threshold:
        flag_for_bias_review(application)
```

This is NOT a scoring component. It's an **audit on the scoring system itself**. The research at lines 292-299 warns:

> **Impossibility**: You cannot simultaneously achieve predictive parity AND equalized odds when base rates differ between groups. You must choose.

We choose **equalized odds** (same false positive/negative rates across regions) because false rejection of a legitimate farmer is worse than false approval of a weak one.

### Putting It Together: Fairness Score Formula

```python
def calculate_fairness(row, district_stats, type_stats):
    # Layer B: Progressive farm size weighting
    size_percentile = percentile_within_type(row.volume, row.subsidy_type)
    if size_percentile > 0.95:  # Creamy layer exclusion
        size_score = 0
    else:
        size_score = 100 * (1 - size_percentile)
    
    # Layer C: Regional equity (FEMA model)
    district_coverage = district_stats[row.district].funding_per_farmer
    national_avg = national_funding_per_farmer
    regional_score = 100 * max(0, 1 - (district_coverage / national_avg))
    
    # Access equity: first-time applicant boost (Singapore HDB model)
    access_score = 100 if row.is_first_time else max(0, 100 - row.retry_count * 15)
    
    # Concentration penalty: inverse HHI (higher HHI = more concentrated = lower score)
    concentration_score = 100 * (1 - district_stats[row.district].hhi)
    
    # Multiplicative (interactions matter):
    # Small farmer in underserved region = multiplicative boost
    fairness = (
        0.35 * size_score +
        0.30 * regional_score +
        0.20 * access_score +
        0.15 * concentration_score
    )
    
    # Interaction bonus: compound disadvantage
    if size_score > 70 and regional_score > 70:
        fairness *= 1.15  # 15% bonus for compound disadvantage
    
    return min(fairness, 100)
```

**Layer A (Leximin) and Layer D (Counterfactual audit) are NOT in the score — they're applied at allocation and audit stages respectively.**

## 3. How to Measure Impact Properly

This is the hardest dimension

### Planner Response

## 3. How to Measure Impact Properly (concise)

**Core problem**: We have NO outcome data. We can't measure what happened after a subsidy was given.

**Best proxies from research:**

| Proxy | Source | Formula |
|-------|--------|---------|
| **Sector gap** | National Concept 2021-2030 | `(target - current) / target` per sector. Poultry gap=42% → highest priority |
| **Regional marginal return** | FEMA Vulnerability Index | Districts with lowest current coverage → highest marginal impact |
| **Multiplier type** | GiveWell cost-effectiveness | Breeding (creates future herd) > Production subsidy (maintains) > One-time acquisition |
| **Regional specialization fit** | Verified patterns doc | `district_type_share / national_type_share`. Camels in Mangystau=9.1x (perfect fit) |

```python
def calculate_impact(row, district_stats):
    sector_gap = SECTOR_GAPS.get(row.subsidy_type, 0.5)  # 0-1
    regional_fit = row.specialization_index / max_specialization  # 0-1
    multiplier = MULTIPLIER_MAP.get(row.subsidy_category, 0.5)  # breeding=1.0, production=0.7, acquisition=0.4
    coverage_gap = 1 - (district_stats[row.district].coverage / national_avg_coverage)
    
    impact = (0.30 * sector_gap + 0.25 * coverage_gap + 
              0.25 * multiplier + 0.20 * regional_fit) * 100
    return min(impact, 100)
```

**Honest limitation**: This is estimated impact, not measured. We state this openly.

## Bottom Line: What Changes from Original Design

| Dimension | Original (too simple) | Upgraded (from research) |
|-----------|----------------------|-------------------------|
| **Fairness** | Binary flags | Continuous progressive + creamy layer exclusion + multiplicative interactions |
| **Impact** | Vague "strategic alignment" | Sector gap × regional fit × multiplier × coverage gap |
| **Anomaly** | Single Isolation Forest | 4-layer: statistical + behavioral + distributional + structural |
| **Allocation** | Pure weighted sum | Leximin floor guarantee → then merit-based ranking |
| **Audit** | None | Counterfactual fairness check post-scoring |
| **Display** | Absolute score | Percentile within category (NIH model) |

## Deep Challenge of All 4 Dimension Engines

---

### 🔴 DIMENSION 1: Approval Likelihood (XGBoost) — FUNDAMENTALLY WRONG

This is the biggest problem. From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/deep-analysis-findings-4e60cc.md:161-165`:

> "Текущий отказ — бинарный (соответствует/нет). **Наша задача — не предсказать отказ, а ранжировать ОДОБРЕННЫХ.**"

**The XGBoost dimension answers the WRONG question.**

| Question | What it measures | Who cares? |
|----------|-----------------|-----------|
| "Will this be rejected?" | Eligibility check prediction | Pre-Check tool (useful, but not merit) |
| "Among approved, who deserves priority?" | **Merit** | **This is the actual task from ТЗ** |

Training on rejection/approval learns the **binary eligibility checker** — does the farmer have the right BIN, land, ISJ registration? That checker already exists in GISS. We're rebuilding it worse (31% accuracy vs GISS's 100%).

**What XGBoost actually learns with district as #1 predictor (0.058 bits):**
"Applications from Pavlodar get approved (0% reject), applications from Almaty get rejected (50% reject)." 

That's **inspector strictness variation**, not application quality. If we use this as a merit dimension, we're saying: "merit = being from a lenient district." That's the opposite of merit.

**Edge case that breaks it completely:**
From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/docs/PATTERNS.md:44-45`:
- Pavlodar: 0% reject rate for code 00400
- Almaty: 50% reject rate for code 00400

A farmer in Almaty with an identical application to a Pavlodar farmer would get a LOWER "approval likelihood" score. But they might be a BETTER farmer who happens to live in a stricter district. Our model would penalize them for their geography.

**Challenge verdict: This dimension should NOT exist as a merit component.**

XGBoost should be used for:
1. **Pre-Check** (separate from scoring): "Your application has characteristics that historically face 40% rejection rate in your district. Common issues: [list]"
2. **Feature importance discovery**: SHAP tells us which features matter — this informs the OTHER dimensions
3. **Input to anomaly detection**: The residuals (cases where model is very wrong) are interesting anomalies

But it should NOT contribute to the composite merit score.

---

### 🟡 DIMENSION 2: Impact Potential — MULTIPLE FORMULA FLAWS

#### Flaw 1: Sector gap requires external data we don't have

```python
sector_gap = SECTOR_GAPS.get(row.subsidy_type, 0.5)
```

Where does `SECTOR_GAPS` come from? Not from our dataset. We'd need to hardcode: "poultry self-sufficiency = 58%, therefore gap = 42%." But these numbers come from the Concept 2021-2030 document, not from the Excel file. We'd be injecting external assumptions as fact.

**If we hardcode wrong numbers, the entire dimension is garbage.**

#### Flaw 2: Regional fit REWARDS existing patterns, not needed diversification

The specialization index from `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/docs/PATTERNS.md:79-84`:
- Mangystau: camels = 9.1x (high fit)
- Mangystau: cattle = 0.1x (low fit)

If we use `regional_fit = specialization_index`, a cattle farmer in Mangystau gets punished. But maybe Mangystau **needs** cattle diversification for food security! High specialization index means "this is what the region already does" — not "this is what the region should keep doing."

**Should high specialization = high fit or low fit?** It depends on whether you want to:
- Reinforce comparative advantage (high fit → good) 
- Encourage diversification (high fit → neutral, low fit → bonus)

These are opposite policies. The formula can't default to one without an explicit policy choice.

#### Flaw 3: Coverage gap creates oscillation

If underfunded districts get higher impact scores → more funding → gap closes → score drops → less funding → gap reopens. In a static snapshot this is fine, but if deployed, it creates boom-bust cycles.

More importantly: **a district might have low funding because it has few farmers, not because it's underserved.** A semi-urban district with 3 farmers and low total funding would score as "high coverage gap" even though it's not an agricultural area at all.

Fix: Use funding per agricultural worker, not total funding. But we don't have agricultural worker counts per district.

#### Flaw 4: No interaction between sector gap and regional fit

```python
# Current: additive
impact = 0.30 * sector_gap + 0.25 * coverage_gap + 0.25 * multiplier + 0.20 * regional_fit

# Problem: Poultry in desert = 0.30 * 0.42 + ... + 0.20 * 0.0 = medium score
# Reality: Poultry in desert = terrible idea = should be LOW
```

A sector with HIGH national gap but ZERO regional fit should score LOW, not medium. These terms should MULTIPLY, not add:

```python
# Better: multiplicative where it matters
effective_gap = sector_gap * (0.5 + 0.5 * regional_fit)  # Gap discounted by fit
```

#### Flaw 5: Multiplier categories are invented

"Breeding = 1.0, production = 0.7, acquisition = 0.4" — these numbers have no empirical basis. We're asserting that breeding creates 2.5x more long-term value than acquisition. Based on what? The PDF rules say breeding cattle must be kept ≥2 years (which implies commitment), but a one-time semen acquisition (code 00100, norm 10,000 tg) could enable genetic improvement across hundreds of cattle. Which has more impact? It depends on the specific case.

---

### 🟡 DIMENSION 3: Fairness Index — 6 EDGE CASES THAT BREAK IT

#### Edge Case 1: Volume has different units per type

From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/verified-patterns-4e60cc.md:122-133`:

> "Когда мы считаем sum/norm — получаем РАЗНЫЕ метрики. Для молока это ТОННЫ, для скота — ГОЛОВЫ."

`size_score = percentile_within_type(volume)` — but 100 tonnes of milk vs 100 heads of cattle are fundamentally different scales. Even within-type percentile ranking doesn't solve this because the MEANING of "small" differs:
- 10 cattle = subsistence farmer (truly small)
- 10,000 kg milk/year = small-medium dairy farm (not subsistence)

Both might be at the 20th percentile within their type but represent completely different realities.

#### Edge Case 2: Access_score penalizes friction VICTIMS

Current: `access_score = 100 if first_time else max(0, 100 - retry_count * 15)`

But from `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/docs/PATTERNS.md:9`:

> "65% отказов — повторные подачи одного фермера (одинаковый район + тип + сумма)."

These retries are farmers who got rejected for **paperwork issues** (wrong documents, missing registration), not because they're bad applicants. Penalizing retry_count means penalizing farmers who struggled with bureaucracy — the exact people fairness should PROTECT.

A farmer who retried 3 times and finally got approved is MORE persistent and dedicated than one who succeeded first try with a consultant's help. Our formula says the opposite.

#### Edge Case 3: Concentration penalty hurts the WRONG people

Current: `concentration_score = 100 * (1 - district_HHI)`

From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/docs/PATTERNS.md:73-74`:
- Tyulkubassky: 86% budget → one producer
- All OTHER farmers in Tyulkubassky also get low concentration_score

A small farmer in Tyulkubassky — who is the **victim** of monopolization — gets penalized by the very metric designed to fight monopolization! The penalty should apply to the dominant producer only, and the small farmers should get a BONUS (they're competing against a monopolist).

This requires knowing WHO the dominant producer is. Without farmer IDs, we can only approximate: if an application's amount is >50% of the district's total, it's likely the dominant producer.

#### Edge Case 4: Creamy layer threshold varies by type

95th percentile cutoff treats all subsidy types equally. But:
- Top 5% of poultry applications: industrial mega-farms (Bulandynsky: 1.2B tenge)
- Top 5% of cattle breeding: medium-large family ranch (~15M tenge)

A 15M tenge cattle ranch is not "creamy layer" in the same sense as a 1.2B tenge poultry factory. The threshold should be TYPE-SPECIFIC:

```python
# Better: type-specific thresholds
if subsidy_type == 'poultry' and percentile > 0.90:  # Industrial threshold
    creamy_layer = True
elif subsidy_type == 'cattle' and percentile > 0.98:  # Only mega-operations
    creamy_layer = True
```

But who decides these thresholds? They're policy decisions, not data-driven.

#### Edge Case 5: Multiplicative interaction bonus is one-directional

Current: `if size_score > 70 and regional_score > 70: fairness *= 1.15`

What about the REVERSE? A large farmer (size_score=10) in a well-funded district (regional_score=10) should get a compound PENALTY, not just low individual scores. Without a compound penalty, a large farmer in a rich district still gets a fairness score of `0.35*10 + 0.30*10 + 0.20*50 + 0.15*50 = 24`. That's low but not zero.

Should it be zero? Is it ever "fair" to give a fairness bonus to a large farm in a rich district? Or should fairness have a floor?

#### Edge Case 6: Gaming via entity splitting

If small farms get higher fairness scores, a 1000-head operation could split into 10 × 100-head entities to get 10 fairness bonuses instead of 1 penalty. Our data shows rapid-fire submissions from the same district — some of these COULD be split entities.

The anomaly dimension is supposed to catch this, but there's a direct tension: Fairness rewards smallness, Anomaly penalizes clusters of similar small applications. These can work against each other.

---

### 🟡 DIMENSION 4: Anomaly Score — HALF THE SIGNALS ARE DEBUNKED

#### Signal 1: Benford's Law — DEBUNKED as fraud indicator

From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/verified-patterns-4e60cc.md:40-54`:

> "Аномалия Бенфорда в Шымкенте — НЕ фрод, а следствие однородной структуры субсидий (почти только молоко)."

Using Benford deviation as an anomaly signal flags ALL of Shymkent (legitimate milk producers). **Remove from scoring, keep as visualization only.**

#### Signal 2: Rapid-fire — DEBUNKED as suspicious

From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/verified-patterns-4e60cc.md:64-68`:

> "Multi-type фермеры имеют reject rate 3.9% vs single-type 9.2%. Фермер, который подаёт на несколько субсидий — более организованный."

Rapid-fire submissions = organized farmer, not fraud. **Remove from anomaly, use for entity linking only.**

#### Signal 3: "Clones" — NORMAL for small farmers

From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/.windsurf/plans/deep-analysis-findings-4e60cc.md:56-61`:

> "Это РАЗНЫЕ фермеры с одинаковым размером хозяйства (одинаковое поголовье × норматив = одинаковая сумма). Нормально для мелких хозяйств."

Kaztalovskiy: 90 applications for 300K tenge = 90 small farmers with ~same herd size. **Not anomalous.**

#### Signal 4: Weekend/night submissions — DEBUNKED

From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/docs/PATTERNS.md:97`:

> "Ночные подачи одобряют чаще — ❌ Часовые пояса. Мангистау UTC+5, система UTC+6"

**Remove from scoring.**

#### What ACTUALLY works for anomaly detection:

After removing debunked signals, we're left with:

| Signal | Still Valid? | Basis |
|--------|------------|-------|
| **Volume outlier within type+oblast** | ✅ | Z-score >3 = genuinely unusual |
| **District monopoly** (single producer >50%) | ✅ | Verified: Tyulkubassky 86% |
| **Withdrawn + large amount** | ✅ | Withdrawn avg 6.08M vs approved 3.5M |
| **Isolation Forest on WITHIN-TYPE features** | ✅ | But must be within-type to avoid flagging legitimate large operations across types |
| **Batch rejection pattern** | ✅ | Zhualynsky: 12/12 rejected same day |
| ~~Benford deviation~~ | ❌ | Debunked: structural, not fraud |
| ~~Rapid-fire~~ | ❌ | Debunked: organized farmers |
| ~~Night/weekend~~ | ❌ | Debunked: timezone artifacts |
| ~~Clone detection~~ | ❌ | Debunked: normal for small farmers |

**Half the anomaly signals are debunked.** The dimension shrinks significantly.

---

### 🔴 CROSS-DIMENSION CHALLENGE: DISTRICT DOMINANCE

**The most fundamental problem across ALL dimensions:**

From `@/Users/kambarmirmanov/Desktop/projects/hackaton/decentrathon5-ai-indrive/docs/PATTERNS.md:28-32`:

```
district  : 0.058 bits  (#1 predictor)
oblast    : 0.041 bits  (#2)
subsidy_code: 0.032 bits (#3)
everything else: noise
```

Every dimension uses district-level features:
- **Impact**: `coverage_gap` = district funding level
- **Fairness**: `regional_score` = district funding per capita, `concentration_score` = district HHI
- **Anomaly**: `district_monopoly`, `batch_rejection`

If district drives everything, the composite score is essentially: **"which district are you in?"** with different mathematical decorations.

A farmer has NO CONTROL over their district. Scoring them primarily by geography means the system is not scoring MERIT but LOCATION. That's the exact opposite of what the ТЗ asks for.

**This is the central tension of the entire project: the data only tells us about geography and subsidy type. Individual farmer merit is invisible.**

---

## REVISED ARCHITECTURE (after all challenges)

```
Stage 0: DATA
  └── Excel → Clean → Feature engineering → Case-level dedup

Stage 1: PRE-CHECK (separate from scoring)
  ├── XGBoost: predict eligibility failure probability
  ├── SHAP: show which factors predict issues
  └── Output: "Your application profile has X% historical approval 
       rate. Common issues in your district: [list]"
  ⚠️ This is a TOOL for farmers, NOT a merit score component

Stage 2: MERIT SCORING (3 dimensions, not 4)
  ├── Impact Potential (upgraded):
  │   ├── Sector gap × regional fit (MULTIPLICATIVE, not additive)
  │   ├── Coverage gap (funding per farmer, not total)
  │   ├── Multiplier type (with type-specific calibration)
  │   └── ⚠️ Explicit: "sector gaps are policy inputs, not data-derived"
  │
  ├── Fairness Index (upgraded):
  │   ├── Size equity: continuous, within-type percentile
  │   ├── Regional equity: funding per farmer vs national avg
  │   ├── Access equity: NEUTRAL on retry (not penalizing)
  │   ├── Concentration correction: 
  │   │   ├── Dominant producer → PENALTY
  │   │   └── Non-dominant in monopoly district → BONUS
  │   ├── Creamy layer: type-specific thresholds
  │   └── Interactions: both compound disadvantage bonus AND compound advantage penalty
  │
  └── Anomaly Score (trimmed):
      ├── Isolation Forest WITHIN subsidy type
      ├── Volume outlier (Z>3 within type+oblast)
      ├── District monopoly (entity-level, not district-level)
      ├── Withdrawn+large pattern
      └── ❌ Removed: Benford, rapid-fire, night/weekend, clones

Stage 3: COMPOSITE (configurable)
  ├── Weighted sum with commission-controlled sliders
  ├── ⚠️ Weights are POLICY DECISIONS, not data-derived
  └── Display as percentile within subsidy type (not absolute score)

Stage 4: ALLOCATION (separate from scoring)
  ├── Leximin floor: every district ≥ minimum
  ├── Budget simulation: FIFO vs Merit vs Custom
  └── Counterfactual audit: does changing district change score significantly?
```

### Key Changes from Previous Design

| What Changed | Why |
|-------------|-----|
| **Removed Dimension 1 (XGBoost) from merit** | Predicts eligibility, not merit. Moved to Pre-Check tool. |
| **Impact: multiplicative, not additive** | Sector gap × regional fit, preventing "poultry in desert" |
| **Fairness: retry-neutral** | Retries = friction victims, not bad applicants |
| **Fairness: entity-level concentration** | Don't penalize small farmers in monopoly districts |
| **Anomaly: removed 4 debunked signals** | Benford, rapid-fire, night/weekend, clones all debunked |
| **Anomaly: within-type Isolation Forest** | Prevents flagging legitimate large operations across types |
| **Explicit: district drives everything** | Acknowledge this as a limitation, not hide it |
| **Explicit: weights are policy, not data** | Don't pretend the formula is objective |

*Asked user a question*

### Planner Response

Let me be completely honest about this.

## What Our Data Actually Contains Per Application

| Feature | Individual or Aggregate? | Can farmer control it? |
|---------|------------------------|----------------------|
| `subsidy_type` | Individual choice | ✅ Yes — farmer chooses what to apply for |
| `volume` (sum/norm) | Individual measurement | ✅ Yes — reflects their actual operation size |
| `district` | Geographic | ❌ No — they live where they live |
| `oblast` | Geographic | ❌ No |
| `submission_date` | Individual behavior | ⚠️ Partially — when they choose to apply |
| `retry_count` (from dedup) | Individual behavior | ⚠️ Reflects bureaucratic navigation |
| `multi_type` (from dedup) | Individual behavior | ✅ Yes — diversified vs single-type farmer |

**Everything derived from district/oblast** (reject rates, HHI, coverage, specialization) is aggregate/geographic. That's where our features MULTIPLY — but they're all repackaging the same geography signal.

## Are We Fooling Ourselves?

**Partially yes, partially no.**

### The "yes" part:

With 36,651 applications, the features that explain rejection variance are:
```
district:      0.058 bits  (geographic)
oblast:        0.041 bits  (geographic)
subsidy_code:  0.032 bits  (type choice)
month:         0.007 bits  (timing)
everything else: noise
```

If we strip away the geographic decorations (coverage_gap, regional_equity, concentration_score, district_monopoly — these are ALL district in different hats), we're left with:

**The only non-geographic individual-level variables are:**
1. **What they apply for** (subsidy type)
2. **How big they are** (volume)
3. **When they apply** (date)
4. **Behavioral pattern** (retries, multi-type, withdrawal)

That's it. Everything else is district repackaged.

### The "no" part:

This isn't unique to us. **Every scoring system with limited data faces this.**

| System | "Individual" data | "Location" data | Still useful? |
|--------|------------------|----------------|--------------|
| Credit scoring | Payment history, utilization | ZIP code (major predictor!) | Yes |
| Car insurance | Driving record, vehicle | ZIP code (major predictor!) | Yes |
| University admissions | GPA, essays | School district (predicts outcomes) | Yes |
| **Our system** | Type, size, behavior | District (major predictor) | **Yes, if honest** |

Nobody calls car insurance "location-based" even though ZIP code is a top-3 predictor. The key: they're transparent about it and combine it with individual variables.

**Our system IS merit-based, but "merit" with this data means: "application profile merit" — not "individual farmer quality merit."**

### What "application profile merit" can actually distinguish:

With 192 districts × ~10 subsidy types × 5 size quintiles × behavioral patterns, we have ~9,600+ distinct profiles. Two examples:

**Profile A**: Small cattle farmer (Q1 volume) in an underserved district, applying for breeding subsidies (strategic), first-time applicant, no anomaly flags.
→ High merit: strategic type + small farmer equity + underserved region + clean profile

**Profile B**: Large industrial operation (Q5 volume) in a concentrated district where they're the dominant producer, applying for feed cost reduction (non-strategic), serial applicant.
→ Low merit: non-strategic type + large operation + monopolist + low marginal impact

These ARE meaningfully different. The system IS ranking them differently. The question is: **is this useful compared to FIFO?**

### FIFO vs Our Scoring: What actually changes?

Under FIFO: Profile A and Profile B are ranked by who filed first. The industrial operation with lawyers files in January. The small farmer files in April when they notice the deadline.

Under our scoring: Profile A ranks higher than Profile B regardless of filing date.

**That IS a real improvement.** It's not perfect individual merit, but it's better than filing-time merit.

## What We Actually CAN'T Do (and must be honest about)

We CANNOT measure:
- **Farm productivity** (no yield/output data)
- **Farmer capability** (no education, experience, compliance data)
- **Subsidy ROI** (no outcome data — what happened after subsidy was granted?)
- **True fraud** (no confirmed fraud labels)
- **Financial need** (no income data)

We MUST NOT pretend to measure these. The ТЗ says:

> "rank applicants based on data (subsidy history, farm characteristics, performance indicators, **and other available features**)"

The key phrase is **"available features."** They know the data is limited.

## So What Should We ACTUALLY Build?

**An honest system that:**

1. **Scores what it CAN score** — application profile merit (type + size + behavioral signals), with geographic context as explicit, adjustable inputs
2. **Separates geographic context from individual signal** — don't blend them into one opaque score. Show: "Your individual profile score is X. Your geographic context modifies it by Y. Commission can adjust how much geography matters."
3. **Acknowledges what it CAN'T score** — "We cannot measure farm productivity, ROI, or farmer capability from this data. These would require integration with ISJ, EASУ, and production reporting systems."
4. **Proves it's still better than FIFO** — The budget simulator shows that even imperfect merit-based allocation produces better distributional outcomes than filing-time ordering.

### Revised Dimension Split: Individual vs Geographic

```
INDIVIDUAL MERIT (farmer can influence):
├── Type Choice Score: strategic alignment of what they apply for
├── Scale Equity Score: relative size (smaller = higher equity)
├── Behavioral Score: first-time, multi-type, retry pattern
└── Anomaly Flag: statistical outlier within type

GEOGRAPHIC CONTEXT (farmer can't influence, commission adjusts weight):
├── Regional Need: coverage gap in their district
├── Concentration Environment: monopoly vs diverse district
└── Budget Pressure: seasonal timing relative to budget cycles

COMPOSITE:
├── individual_merit × (1 - geo_weight) + geographic_context × geo_weight
├── geo_weight default: 0.4 (commission adjustable 0.0 to 0.6)
└── Display: "Score: 72 (individual: 78, geographic adjustment: -6)"
```

**The critical difference**: Instead of hiding geography inside every dimension, we SEPARATE it and let the commission decide how much it matters. If they set `geo_weight=0`, it's pure individual profile scoring. If they set it to 0.4, geography moderates the score.

This is more honest, more useful, and more defensible to judges than pretending geography isn't the dominant signal.

### Planner Response

Let me re-read the exact ТЗ language on this. From what the user pasted earlier:

**Section 5, Case 2:**
> *"All calculations, models, and conclusions must be based on the provided data. **Additional open sources may be used to enrich the model, provided those sources are explicitly referenced in the solution.**"*

**Section 8:**
> *"Participants must use this dataset as the **primary basis** for developing the scoring model."*

## This Changes Everything

The dataset is the PRIMARY basis, but we **CAN use external open data** — we just have to reference it.

### What Open Data Exists for Kazakhstan Agriculture

| Source | What it has | URL | What it unlocks |
|--------|-----------|-----|----------------|
| **stat.gov.kz** | Livestock counts by region, farm counts, agricultural production by oblast | Open | REAL sector gaps, REAL regional coverage, funding per farm |
| **data.egov.kz** | Government open data, budget allocations | Open | Budget per region, subsidy program budgets |
| **Concept 2021-2030** | National agricultural targets | Published policy doc | REAL sector gap targets (poultry, beef, dairy) |
| **MoA reports** | Annual agricultural production statistics | Published | Self-sufficiency ratios by product type |

### What We CANNOT Get (not open)

| Source | Why not | What it would add |
|--------|---------|------------------|
| **ISJ (livestock registry)** | Restricted government system | Individual farm herd composition, breeding history |
| **Tax authority** | Restricted | Farm income, tax compliance |
| **GISS** (subsidy system) | Restricted | Full subsidy history per farmer |
| **EASУ** | Restricted | Land ownership, agricultural land per farmer |
| **Credit history** | Restricted (First Credit Bureau) | Financial health |

### What This Means for Our Dimensions

**With external open data enrichment, we can compute REAL versions of currently-fake proxies:**

| Metric | Currently (dataset only) | With open enrichment |
|--------|------------------------|---------------------|
| **Sector gap** | Hardcoded guess | Real: `(Concept_2030_target - stat.gov.kz_current) / target` |
| **Regional need** | Relative within our dataset | Real: `stat.gov.kz_farms_per_district / funding_per_district` |
| **Funding per farmer** | Only from our 36K applications | Real: total agricultural budget per region / registered farms |
| **Livestock density** | `sum/norm` from applications only | Real: stat.gov.kz livestock census per district |
| **Specialization index** | Derived from our subsidy mix | Validated against actual production mix from stat.gov.kz |

**This makes Impact Potential and Fairness dimensions MUCH more grounded.** They'd be based on national statistics, not just patterns within our 36K application sample.

### But Individual Farmer Merit Still Requires Restricted Data

Even with open enrichment, we **still can't score**:
- Past subsidy performance (did they use the money well?)
- Tax compliance
- Farm productivity (yield per hectare, milk per cow)
- Financial need (income level)

These require ISJ, GISS, tax authority integration — which is restricted.

### The Honest Architecture: 3 Tiers

```
TIER 1: WHAT WE BUILD NOW (dataset + open enrichment)
├── Application profile scoring (type, size, behavior)
├── Geographic context (real stats from stat.gov.kz)
├── Anomaly detection (from dataset patterns)
└── Budget simulator (FIFO vs Merit comparison)

TIER 2: WHAT THE SYSTEM COULD DO (with ISJ/GISS integration)
├── Individual farm scoring (herd composition, breeding quality)
├── Subsidy history scoring (past performance, compliance)
├── Financial health assessment (tax data)
└── Outcome tracking (did subsidy produce results?)

TIER 3: WHAT THE SYSTEM SHOULD EVENTUALLY DO (full ecosystem)
├── Uplift modeling (with outcome data from Tier 2)
├── Predictive analytics (which subsidies produce best ROI)
├── Automated eligibility checking (real-time GISS integration)
└── Cross-program optimization (across all subsidy types)
```

**For the hackathon**: We build Tier 1 fully, DESIGN Tier 2's schema and show what it would look like, and DESCRIBE Tier 3 as the roadmap.

This is actually a STRONGER presentation strategy: "Here's what we built with available data. Here's what becomes possible with ISJ integration. Here's the full vision." It shows we understand the real system architecture.

## To Answer Your Core Question

**The ТЗ explicitly says:**
> *"Additional open sources may be used to enrich the model, provided those sources are explicitly referenced in the solution."*

So **YES, we can and SHOULD use external data.** The provided dataset is the "primary basis" but not the only source.

### What External Data Breaks Us Through the Wall

| Data Source | What it gives us | What dimension it fixes |
|-------------|-----------------|----------------------|
| **stat.gov.kz livestock census** | Real livestock counts per oblast/district | Impact: REAL sector gaps, not guesses |
| **stat.gov.kz farm counts** | Number of registered farms per district | Fairness: REAL funding-per-farmer ratios |
| **stat.gov.kz production volumes** | Milk/meat/eggs production by region | Impact: REAL self-sufficiency ratios |
| **National Concept 2021-2030** (published doc) | Target production levels by sector | Impact: REAL gap = target - current |
| **MoA budget reports** | Subsidy budget allocations by region | Fairness: REAL budget coverage ratios |

### What We Still CAN'T Get (restricted systems)

| System | What it would add | Why we can't |
|--------|------------------|-------------|
| **ISJ** (livestock registry) | Individual herd composition, breed quality, breeding history | Requires government API access |
| **GISS** (subsidy system) | Past subsidy history per farmer, compliance records | Restricted |
| **Tax authority** | Farm revenue, tax compliance | Restricted |
| **EASУ** | Land ownership, agricultural land area per farmer | Restricted |
| **ИБСПР** (breeding registry) | Breeding program participation, genetics quality | Restricted |

### The Real Question: Can We Check Subsidy History?

From the ТЗ, Case 2 description:
> *"rank applicants based on data (**subsidy history**, farm characteristics, performance indicators)"*

They MENTION "subsidy history" as a ranking factor. But our dataset is a **single snapshot of 2025 applications**. It doesn't contain historical data across years.

HOWEVER — within our dataset, case-level deduplication DOES reveal per-entity behavioral history:

```
From 36,651 applications → 23,851 unique cases:
├── 88.1% approved on first try (no history)
├── 5.8% approved after 1+ retries (has retry history)
├── 2.5% withdrew (has withdrawal history)  
└── 3.6% never approved (has rejection history)

For multi-type farmers (same district, multiple subsidy types):
├── 3.9% reject rate (vs 9.2% overall)
├── These farmers are MORE sophisticated
└── This IS individual-level behavioral data
```

**So our dataset contains SOME individual behavioral history — it's just compressed into a single year's applications.** We can extract:

1. **Retry behavior**: How many attempts? Time between attempts?
2. **Multi-type diversification**: Does this entity apply for multiple subsidy types?
3. **Withdrawal pattern**: Did they start and pull back?
4. **Total portfolio**: Sum across all applications from the same entity
5. **Application sophistication**: Exact multiple of norm (organized) vs non-multiple (1.9% of cases — unusual)

### Revised View: What Individual Merit We CAN Measure

| Signal | Source | Individual? | Controllable? |
|--------|--------|------------|---------------|
| **Subsidy type choice** | Dataset | ✅ Yes | ✅ Farmer decides what to apply for |
| **Operation scale** (volume) | Dataset | ✅ Yes | ✅ Reflects their actual farm |
| **Multi-type diversification** | Dataset (dedup) | ✅ Yes | ✅ Farmer chose to diversify |
| **Retry resilience** | Dataset (dedup) | ✅ Yes | ⚠️ Partially (friction vs persistence) |
| **Application precision** (exact norm multiple) | Dataset | ✅ Yes | ✅ Reflects documentation quality |
| **Withdrawal pattern** | Dataset | ✅ Yes | ✅ Conscious decision |
| **Relative size within type+district** | Dataset | ✅ Yes | ✅ Relative position among peers |
| **Regional livestock density** | stat.gov.kz | ❌ Geographic | ❌ Context, not individual |
| **Sector self-sufficiency gap** | stat.gov.kz + Concept 2030 | ❌ National | ❌ Context, not individual |
| **District farm count** | stat.gov.kz | ❌ Geographic | ❌ Context, not individual |

**We have 7 individual signals and 3 geographic context signals.** That's more individual merit than I initially claimed.

### The Architecture Implication

This means the Individual vs Geographic split becomes:

```
INDIVIDUAL MERIT (7 signals):
├── Type Choice Score
│   ├── Strategic alignment of subsidy type (from Concept 2030 targets)
│   ├── Acquisition vs Production vs Breeding (long-term commitment indicator)
│   └── Multiplier category (national priority sector)
│
├── Scale & Equity Score  
│   ├── Volume percentile within type (continuous, not binary)
│   ├── Creamy layer exclusion (type-specific thresholds)
│   └── Relative size within district+type (are they the big fish or small?)
│
├── Behavioral Score (from case-level dedup)
│   ├── Multi-type diversification (positive: sophisticated farmer)
│   ├── Application precision (exact norm multiple = organized)
│   ├── Withdrawal history (signals self-awareness or gaming)
│   └── Total portfolio across types (scale of total involvement)
│
└── Anomaly Flags (within-type)
    ├── Volume Z-score > 3 within type+oblast
    ├── Entity-level monopoly (>50% of district funding)
    └── Isolation Forest within type

GEOGRAPHIC CONTEXT (3 signals, enriched from stat.gov.kz):
├── Regional Need
│   ├── District livestock density vs national average (stat.gov.kz)
│   ├── District farm count vs funding allocated (stat.gov.kz + dataset)
│   └── Budget pressure timing (dataset temporal patterns)
│
├── Sector Gap
│   ├── National target vs current production (Concept 2030 + stat.gov.kz)
│   └── Regional specialization fit (dataset + stat.gov.kz validation)
│
└── Concentration Environment
    ├── District HHI (dataset)
    └── Entity-level dominance pattern (dataset dedup)

COMPOSITE:
├── Score = individual_weight × Individual + geo_weight × Geographic
├── Default: 60% individual / 40% geographic
├── Commission adjustable via sliders
└── Anomaly flags shown separately (not in composite)
```

### Does This Break Through the Wall?

**Yes, partially:**
- Individual merit now has 7 real signals, not just "location + size"
- Geographic context is grounded in real statistics, not dataset proxies
- The split is transparent: judges see exactly how much is individual vs geographic
- The behavioral signals from case-level dedup are genuinely novel — few teams will do this

**The remaining wall:**
- We still can't measure farm productivity, compliance history, or financial health
- But we DESIGN the schema showing what Tier 2 would look like with ISJ/GISS integration
- This is honest and the ТЗ acknowledges data limitations exist

## Designing Tier 1 → Tier 2 → Tier 3 Scalability

The key pattern is **Feature Provider abstraction** — the scoring engine never touches raw data directly. It consumes standardized features from pluggable providers.

### The Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    FEATURE PROVIDERS (pluggable)              │
│                                                               │
│  Tier 1 (now)          Tier 1 (enrichment)    Tier 2 (future)│
│  ┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────┐  │
│  │ Excel Dataset│     │ stat.gov.kz  │     │ ISJ Registry │  │
│  │ Provider     │     │ Provider     │     │ Provider     │  │
│  │              │     │              │     │              │  │
│  │ • herd_size  │     │ • oblast_    │     │ • real_herd  │  │
│  │   (estimated)│     │   livestock  │     │ • breed_qual │  │
│  │ • type_code  │     │ • farm_count │     │ • bull_ratio │  │
│  │ • district   │     │ • production │     │ • age_distrib│  │
│  │ • volume     │     │ • self_suff  │     │ • breeding_  │  │
│  │ • retry_cnt  │     │   iciency    │     │   program    │  │
│  └──────┬───────┘     └──────┬───────┘     └──────┬───────┘  │
│         │                    │                     │          │
│  ┌──────┴────────────────────┴─────────────────────┴───────┐  │
│  │              UNIFIED FEATURE STORE                       │  │
│  │                                                          │  │
│  │  Per application: { feature_name → (value, source,       │  │
│  │                      confidence, tier, category) }       │  │
│  │                                                          │  │
│  │  Rule: When multiple providers supply same feature,      │  │
│  │        use highest confidence. Tier 2 overrides Tier 1.  │  │
│  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┘  │
│                         │                                      │
│  ┌──────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┐  │
│  │              SCORING ENGINE                               │  │
│  │                                                           │  │
│  │  Consumes FEATURES, not raw data.                         │  │
│  │  Dimensions activate/deactivate based on available data.  │  │
│  │  Output includes confidence based on data completeness.   │  │
│  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### How It Works in Practice

**Core principle: Same feature name, different sources, automatic upgrade.**

```python
# Tier 1: herd_size estimated from subsidy amount / norm rate
Feature("herd_size", value=app.sum/app.norm, source="dataset", confidence=0.70)

# Tier 2: herd_size from ISJ livestock registry (real count)
Feature("herd_size", value=isj.get_herd(bin), source="isj", confidence=0.99)
```

The scoring engine reads `features["herd_size"].value` — it doesn't know or care where it came from. When ISJ becomes available, the value automatically upgrades. **Zero scoring code changes.**

### Concrete Feature Map: What Exists Now vs What Plugs In Later

| Feature | Tier 1 Source | Tier 1 Confidence | Tier 2 Source | Tier 2 Confidence |
|---------|-------------|-------------------|--------------|-------------------|
| `herd_size` | `sum/norm` from Excel | 0.70 | ISJ registry | 0.99 |
| `farm_land_area` | ❌ Not available | — | EASУ land registry | 0.95 |
| `breed_quality` | ❌ Not available | — | ИБСПР breeding registry | 0.95 |
| `past_subsidies_count` | Case-level dedup (partial) | 0.50 | GISS full history | 0.99 |
| `past_compliance_rate` | ❌ Not available | — | GISS + Form 1-ВПЖ | 0.95 |
| `tax_compliance` | ❌ Not available | — | Tax authority API | 0.99 |
| `oblast_livestock_total` | ❌ Not available | — | stat.gov.kz (Tier 1!) | 0.95 |
| `district_farm_count` | Estimated from dataset | 0.60 | stat.gov.kz (Tier 1!) | 0.90 |
| `sector_self_sufficiency` | Hardcoded from Concept | 0.50 | stat.gov.kz + MoA | 0.90 |
| `subsidy_type` | Excel column | 1.00 | Same | 1.00 |
| `district` | Excel column | 1.00 | Same | 1.00 |
| `is_multi_type_farmer` | Case-level dedup | 0.80 | GISS entity linking | 0.99 |
| `retry_count` | Case-level dedup | 0.75 | GISS submission logs | 0.99 |

### How Scoring Dimensions Scale

```
TIER 1 (NOW) — 3 active scoring dimensions:
├── Type Choice Score        [3 features, confidence ~0.75]
├── Scale & Equity Score     [4 features, confidence ~0.65]  
└── Behavioral Score         [4 features, confidence ~0.70]
    
    Overall score confidence: ~0.70
    Message: "Score based on application data. 3/5 dimensions active."

TIER 2 (WITH ISJ/GISS) — 5 active scoring dimensions:
├── Type Choice Score        [3 features, confidence ~0.75]  (same)
├── Scale & Equity Score     [6 features, confidence ~0.90]  (+ real herd, land)
├── Behavioral Score         [4 features, confidence ~0.90]  (+ real history)
├── Farm Quality Score  [NEW][4 features, confidence ~0.95]  (breed, bull ratio, etc.)
└── Track Record Score  [NEW][3 features, confidence ~0.95]  (compliance, past ROI)
    
    Overall score confidence: ~0.90
    Message: "Score based on verified registry data. 5/5 dimensions active."
```

**The scoring engine code doesn't change between Tier 1 and Tier 2.** New dimensions activate automatically when their required features appear in the Feature Store.

### The Implementation Pattern (Python)

```python
# feature_store.py
class FeatureStore:
    def __init__(self, providers: list):
        self.providers = providers
    
    def get_features(self, application) -> dict:
        """Merge features from all providers. Higher confidence wins."""
        merged = {}
        for provider in self.providers:
            for name, feature in provider.extract(application).items():
                if name not in merged or feature.confidence > merged[name].confidence:
                    merged[name] = feature
        return merged

# scoring_engine.py  
class ScoringEngine:
    def score(self, features: dict) -> CompositeScore:
        dimensions = {}
        
        # Always available (Tier 1)
        dimensions["type_choice"] = self._score_type_choice(features)
        dimensions["scale_equity"] = self._score_scale_equity(features)
        dimensions["behavioral"] = self._score_behavioral(features)
        
        # Activates when ISJ data available (Tier 2)
        if "breed_quality" in features and "bull_to_cow_ratio" in features:
            dimensions["farm_quality"] = self._score_farm_quality(features)
        
        # Activates when GISS history available (Tier 2)
        if "past_compliance_rate" in features:
            dimensions["track_record"] = self._score_track_record(features)
        
        # Confidence-weighted composite
        total_score = sum(d.score * d.confidence for d in dimensions.values())
        total_weight = sum(d.confidence for d in dimensions.values())
        
        return CompositeScore(
            value=total_score / total_weight,
            dimensions=dimensions,
            active_count=len(dimensions),
            max_count=5,
            confidence=total_weight / len(dimensions),
        )
```

**Slide 4**: "The scoring engine code is IDENTICAL between slides 1-3. Only the data providers change."

This is a **very strong technical story**: we designed for production from day one. The hackathon demo uses available data, but the architecture is ready for the real government system.

Most hackathon teams will:

- Build a model that only works with that specific dataset
- Have no story for "how does this work in production?"

We show:
- Pluggable data providers
- Automatic dimension activation
- Confidence-weighted scoring
- Clear upgrade path from hackathon → pilot → production

## Complete AS IS Process — Fully Decoded

This diagram is a **goldmine**. It reveals 8 steps, 7 external system integrations, and the exact FIFO insertion point. Here's the complete process:

### 5 Swim Lanes
1. **Услугополучатель (СХТП)** — Farmer
2. **ИСС (subsidy.plem.kz) / МСХ** — Subsidy system / Ministry
3. **УСХ специалист** — Local agriculture department specialist
4. **УСХ бухгалтер** — Accountant
5. **Казначейство** — Treasury

### The 8-Step Process

| Step | What Happens | System Checked | Human? |
|------|-------------|---------------|--------|
| **0. Registration** | Farmer registers/authorizes in subsidy.plem.kz. Window: Jan 20 – Dec 20 | ИСС | ❌ Auto |
| **1. Application** | Selects subsidy type + local МИО. System checks counter-obligations | ГИСС (gosagro.kz) + ИАС "РОЖ" | ❌ Auto |
| **2. Livestock verification** | System verifies livestock data by quantity, parameters, age. **Checks for previously received subsidies on SAME ANIMALS** | ИС "ИСЖ" + ИАС "РОЖ" | ❌ Auto |
| **3. Cost indication** | Farmer specifies cost per animal. Import path differs | ИСС | ❌ Auto |
| **4. Invoice verification** | System checks: total ESF ≥ total subsidy amount? Checks ЕАСУ registration | ИС "ЭРОФ" + ЕАСУ | ❌ Auto |
| **5. Land verification** | Checks agricultural land ownership. SPK cooperative path differs | ЕГКН | ❌ Auto |
| **6. Document upload** | Farmer uploads: payment proof, quarantine act, purchase agreement, customs docs (imports). Signs with EDS → **APPLICATION ADDED TO WAITING LIST** | ИСС | ❌ Auto |
| **7. Specialist review** | **ONLY HUMAN STEP.** Reviews documents. Binary: correct → approved, incorrect → motivated refusal | ИСС | ✅ Human |
| **8. Budget & Payment** | Checks budget availability. Full payment / partial payment proposal / stays in waiting list. XML → Treasury → payment | ИС "Клиент-Казначейство" | ⚠️ Semi-auto |

### 7 External Systems Integration Map

```
ИСС (subsidy.plem.kz) ─── Main subsidy portal
ГИСС (gosagro.kz)     ─── Counter-obligations / statistical data
ИАС "РОЖ"             ─── Livestock ACCOUNTING system
ИС "ИСЖ"              ─── Livestock IDENTIFICATION system
ЕАСУ                   ─── Entity registration
ЕГКН                   ─── Land cadastral registry
ИС "ЭРОФ"             ─── Electronic invoices
ИС "Клиент-Казначейство" ── Treasury payments
```

### Critical Findings

**1. The 69% unexplained rejection variance is NOW EXPLAINED.**

Steps 1-5 are ALL automated checks against external systems. Our dataset only shows the final status (approved/rejected) but NOT which automated check failed. The "unexplained" rejections are:
- Step 2: Livestock data mismatch with ИС "ИСЖ"
- Step 2: **Same animals already subsidized** (fraud check!)
- Step 4: Invoice amount doesn't match subsidy amount
- Step 5: No agricultural land in ЕГКН
- Step 7: Specialist found document issues

**2. The FIFO queue insertion point is AFTER Step 6, BEFORE Step 7.**

```
Steps 0-6 (automated checks) → ID assigned → WAITING LIST (FIFO) → Specialist reviews
```

**Our scoring system would replace the FIFO ordering of the waiting list.** Applications still go through all automated checks first. Our system ranks them WITHIN the waiting list.

**3. Partial payments exist.**

> "Если услугополучатель согласился в течение 2 рабочих дней, производится частичная выплата субсидии. Остаток подлежит выплате при перераспределении или при поступлении средств в будущем."

The budget simulator needs to model PARTIAL allocations, not just full-or-nothing.

**4. The fraud check we wanted to build ALREADY EXISTS in Step 2.**

> "Проверка на ранее полученные субсидии по данным животным" = Check for previously received subsidies on THESE SAME ANIMALS

The system already prevents double-subsidizing the same livestock. Our anomaly detection should COMPLEMENT this with pattern-level detection (suspicious districts, volume outliers), not replicate per-animal checks.

### How This Changes Our Architecture

**Before (our assumption):**
```
Application → Black box → Approved/Rejected
```

**After (reality from diagram):**
```
Application → 6 automated checks (ИСЖ, РОЖ, ЕАСУ, ЕГКН, ЭРОФ, ГИСС)
           → WAITING LIST (FIFO)
           → Specialist document review
           → Budget check (full/partial/wait)
           → Treasury payment
```

**Our system inserts at the WAITING LIST step:**
```
Application → 6 automated checks → WAITING LIST → ⭐ OUR RANKING ⭐ → Specialist review
                                                    (instead of FIFO)
```

### Tier 2 Integration Is Now Concrete

We now know EXACTLY which systems to integrate with:

| System | What we get | Feature for scoring |
|--------|-----------|-------------------|
| **ИС "ИСЖ"** | Individual livestock data per farmer | `herd_composition`, `breed_quality`, `animal_age_distribution` |
| **ИАС "РОЖ"** | Livestock accounting | `registered_herd_size`, `herd_growth_trend` |
| **ЕАСУ** | Entity registration | `entity_type`, `registration_date`, `active_status` |
| **ЕГКН** | Land plots | `total_land_area`, `agricultural_land_area` |
| **ГИСС** | Counter-obligations | `past_compliance`, `statistical_reporting_status` |
| **ИС "ЭРОФ"** | Invoice history | `purchase_history`, `supplier_diversity` |

This is the exact Tier 2 Feature Provider spec we were designing abstractly. Now we have concrete system names, data types, and integration points.

### Planner Response

## Second Pass — Additional Findings

### NEW Finding 1: SPK Cooperative Path Is Completely Separate

The diagram shows a parallel workflow for agricultural cooperatives (СПК):
- Decision: "Используются земельные участки СПК?" → branches into a different flow
- For cooperatives: "Вводит все ИИН/БИН членов СК" — all member IDs entered
- ЕГКН checks land for EACH member separately
- Additional document: "Документ, подтверждающий участие в СПК"

**Implication**: A cooperative represents MULTIPLE farmers under one application. Our scoring system needs to handle this — should a cooperative's "small farmer" metric apply to the cooperative entity or to individual members?

### NEW Finding 2: Import Path Has Different Requirements

Import applications (livestock from other countries) require:
- "Таможенная декларация на товары" (customs declaration)
- "Заявление о ввозе товаров и уплате косвенных налогов" (tax payment declaration)
- Separate ESF selection from ИС "ЭРОФ"

**Implication**: Import vs domestic is a meaningful distinction for scoring. Importing breeding stock could be strategically valuable (genetic diversity) or a risk factor (foreign supply dependency). Our `is_acquisition` feature should distinguish domestic vs import.

### NEW Finding 3: Budget Feedback Loop

When budget is insufficient:
```
Application approved → Budget check FAILS → 
  "Заявка остаётся в листе ожидания" (stays in waiting list) →
  Timer: "Ожидание доп. финансирования" (waiting for additional financing) →
  Check: "Средства поступили?" (funds received?) →
  If no → loop back to waiting
  If yes → proceed to payment
```

**Implication**: Applications don't just get rejected for budget reasons — they WAIT. The waiting list is a living queue. This means our ranking system would determine the ORDER in which waiting applications get funded when new budget arrives.

### NEW Finding 4: Partial Payment = Budget Optimization Opportunity

The partial payment note (now clearly readable):
> "Если услугополучатель согласился в течение 2 рабочих дней, производится частичная выплата субсидии. Остаток подлежит выплате при перераспределении или при поступлении средств в будущем."

**Implication**: The budget simulator should model 3 allocation modes:
1. **Full payment** — enough budget for entire subsidy
2. **Partial payment** — farmer gets part now, rest later
3. **Queue** — no budget, farmer waits

This is more nuanced than our "FIFO vs Merit" comparison. We should show: with merit-based ordering, the SAME budget serves MORE farmers through smarter partial allocation.

### NEW Finding 5: Payment Flow Goes Through Treasury

The payment chain: Specialist generates XML → Accountant uploads to "ИС Клиент-Казначейство" → Treasury processes → Form 5-15 reconciliation → Status updated to "Заявка оплачена."

**Implication**: This is purely for the Tier 2/3 roadmap — real-time status tracking integration.

### Summary of ALL Findings (Both Passes)

| Finding | Source | Impact on Our System |
|---------|--------|---------------------|
| 7 external system integrations | Diagram | Tier 2 integration map is now concrete |
| 6 automated pre-checks | Diagram | Explains 69% "unexplained" rejection variance |
| FIFO insertion at Step 6→7 | Diagram | Confirms exact system integration point |
| Previous subsidy check on same animals | Diagram Step 2 | Our fraud detection complements, doesn't replicate |
| SPK cooperative parallel path | Diagram | Need cooperative-aware scoring |
| Import vs domestic distinction | Diagram | New feature for type choice scoring |
| Partial payments exist | Diagram Step 8 | Budget simulator needs 3-mode allocation |
| Budget waiting loop | Diagram Step 8 | Our ranking determines who gets funded when new budget arrives |
| Form 5-15 reconciliation | Diagram | Tier 2: outcome tracking via treasury data |
| EDS (ЭЦП) at multiple stages | Diagram | Security architecture for future deployment |

### What We Didn't Find (confirming first pass)

- ❌ No scoring or ranking in the current process — confirmed pure FIFO
- ❌ No commission review for this subsidy type — only specialist document check
- ❌ No merit-based criteria at any stage — purely eligibility (pass/fail)

The annotation text at the top contained the specific eligibility criteria (cattle age ranges, bull-to-cow ratios) which align with what we found in the PDF rules — no new information there.

### Planner Response

## Key Findings from the PDF Rules Document

This is 116 pages of the actual law governing these subsidies. Here are the critical extracts:

### 1. FIFO Confirmed in Law (Пункт 21)

> "Рассмотрение заявок и выплата субсидий по одобренным заявкам осуществляются **в порядке очередности согласно дате и времени регистрации заявок**."

FIFO is not just a technical limitation — it's codified in law. Our system proposes to **replace this ordering** with merit-based ranking. This needs to be framed carefully: we're not overriding the law, we're proposing a policy improvement for the commission to consider.

### 2. Applications Accepted Regardless of Budget (Пункт 19)

> "Прием заявок осуществляется **вне зависимости от наличия бюджетных средств**"

The waiting list grows INFINITELY even when there's no money. This makes merit-based ordering even MORE critical — the list is always longer than the budget.

### 3. Counter-Obligations Formula (Пункт 14-1) — THIS IS HUGE

Starting 2025, every subsidy recipient must report production volume:

```
Production Volume = quantity_produced (tons/heads/doses) × price_per_unit (₸ thousands)
```

**If production declines for 2 consecutive years → banned from subsidies for 1-2 years.**

This means:
- **The government ALREADY tracks production outcomes** (via Form 1, Приложение 4-1)
- **This data exists in ГИСС** — it's the counter-obligation registry
- **For Tier 2**: This is the EXACT outcome variable we need for impact measurement
- **For Tier 1**: We can proxy it — repeat applicants in our data likely passed the counter-obligation check (they're still eligible)

### 4. 50% Cap on Subsidy Amount (Приложение 1)

> "Приобретение племенных животных субсидируется **до утверждённого норматива, но не более 50% стоимости** от его стоимости приобретения."

Our scoring doesn't change the AMOUNT (calculated automatically from norms). It changes the **ORDER** of who gets funded first. The amount is fixed by law.

### 5. Regional Discretion Is Legally Backed

Some subsidies are explicitly:
> "**за счёт бюджетных средств из местного бюджета по усмотрению МИО, с учётом особенностей и специализации регионов**"

Translation: "at the discretion of local authorities, considering regional characteristics and specialization."

**This is legal cover for our regional scoring dimensions.** The law explicitly says local authorities SHOULD consider regional specialization when allocating certain subsidies.

### 6. Concrete Subsidy Norms (Приложение 1)

| Type | Norm per Unit | Unit |
|------|-------------|------|
| Breeding cattle (КРС) acquisition | Up to 50% of cost, max norm | Per head |
| Breeding rams/ewes | Specific per type | Per head |
| Breeding bulls (semen) | Up to 50% of cost, max 10,000₸/dose | Per dose |
| Poultry (day-old chicks) | Specific norm | Per head |
| Breeding pigs | 100,000₸ | Per head |
| Breeding camels | 175,000₸ | Per head |
| Milk production subsidy | 60₸ (mare milk), 55₸ (camel milk) | Per kg |
| Honey production | 200₸ | Per kg |
| Feed cost subsidy | Set by МИО | Per head (emergency only) |

### 7. Specific Breeding Ratios Required by Law

| Animal | Ratio | Implication |
|--------|-------|-------------|
| Bulls to cows | 1:20–30 (natural) or 1:300 (AI) | System checks this automatically |
| Rams to ewes | 1:20–30 (natural) or 1:300 (AI) or 1:100 (backup) | System checks automatically |
| Boars to sows | 1:50–200 | System checks automatically |
| Camel bulls to females | 1:20–30 | System checks automatically |

These ratios are checked in Step 2 by ИС "ИСЖ". If the farmer doesn't meet the ratio, the application is auto-rejected.

### 8. Special Commission for Poultry (Пункт 17-18)

Poultry operations require:
- Special commission approval (3+ specialists inspect)
- Infrastructure verification every 3 years
- Annual monitoring with photo/video
- HACCP or ISO certification
- Commission can revoke approval

**Poultry applications go through a fundamentally different process** than cattle/sheep. Our model should separate these.

### 9. Force Majeure Exemption

If production drops due to:
- Kazhydromet-confirmed weather events
- Disease outbreaks (quarantine)
- Pest damage

The counter-obligation failure is excused. **Our anomaly detection should NOT flag farmers in regions with declared emergencies.**

### 10. 2-Year Usage Commitment

All breeding stock must be used for ≥2 years. This validates our "multiplier" concept — breeding subsidies have long-term commitment requirements, which IS higher impact than one-time production subsidies.

---

## What This Changes for Our Architecture

| Finding | Impact |
|---------|--------|
| **Counter-obligations exist in ГИСС** | Tier 2 can access REAL production outcome data. This is the gold standard for impact measurement. |
| **FIFO is LAW** | Must frame our system as "commission advisory tool" not "replacement for legal ordering" |
| **Regional discretion is legal** | Our regional scoring dimensions have legal backing |
| **50% cap on amount** | Scoring changes ORDER, not AMOUNT. Simplifies budget simulator. |
| **Breeding ratios auto-checked** | Don't replicate in anomaly detection — already handled by ИСЖ |
| **Poultry is separate process** | Need subsidy-type-aware scoring paths |
| **Force majeure exemptions** | Anomaly detection needs emergency region exclusion |
| **Production volume formula exists** | For Tier 2: `impact = Δ(quantity × price)` between years |

We now have **literally everything** — the dataset, the process diagram, the legal framework, the research, and the challenged dimension formulas.

### Planner Response

## Critical New Findings from Pages 15-17

### Пункт 24-1: Partial Payment → Next in Queue

> "При отклонении товаропроизводителем предложения о частичной выплате или по истечению 2 (двух) рабочих дней **предложение направляется следующему товаропроизводителю согласно листу ожидания**."

If a farmer rejects partial payment, **the money goes to the NEXT person in the FIFO queue**. With merit-based ordering, it would go to the next highest-scoring applicant instead. This is a concrete mechanism where our scoring directly changes outcomes.

### Пункт 24-2: Applications Carry Over Across Fiscal Years

> "В случае отсутствия дополнительных бюджетных средств в текущем финансовом году, по заявкам, поступившим в лист ожидания, **выплата субсидий осуществляется в следующем(их) финансовом(ых) году(ах)**."

Waiting list applications DON'T expire — they carry over to next year. The queue is perpetual.

### Пункт 25: QUARTERLY LIVESTOCK MONITORING + FUND RETURNS — THIS IS THE BIGGEST FIND

> "РОСХ посредством ГИСС **ежеквартально осуществляют мониторинг сохранности просубсидированного поголовья**."

District agriculture departments (РОСХ) do **quarterly monitoring** of every subsidized animal through GISS. This is tracked in a section called **"Мониторинг исполнения обязательств"** (Monitoring of obligation fulfillment).

**If a farmer violates their obligation** (livestock not preserved, excluding natural mortality):
1. РОСХ sends a **return notice** within 30 business days
2. Farmer must **return the subsidy amount per non-preserved animal**
3. Return within **90 business days**
4. If farmer doesn't pay → **court proceedings**
5. **Farmer is BLOCKED from new subsidy applications** until they return the money

> "товаропроизводитель имеет возможность подать новую заявку **только после выполнения своих предыдущих обязательств** (после возврата субсидий за несохранённое поголовье)."

### Why This Is Huge for Our System

| Finding | Impact |
|---------|--------|
| **Quarterly monitoring data exists in GISS** | Tier 2: The BEST individual-level feature — farmers who keep their livestock vs those who don't |
| **Return of funds records exist** | Tier 2: `has_returned_funds` = strongest fraud/non-compliance signal |
| **Blocked farmers exist** | Even in Tier 1: if a farmer has outstanding return obligations, they CAN'T apply. Our pre-check could flag this. |
| **Partial payment cascades down the queue** | Our ranking directly determines who gets partial payment offers when budget is tight |
| **Perpetual waiting list** | Merit ordering is even more critical since the queue never clears |

Let me now check the remaining critical pages — budget redistribution and any rules about how MIO redistributes between subsidy types.

## Found the Formal Rejection Grounds (Пункт 9 Перечня)

Three formal legal bases for rejection:
1. **Недостоверность документов** — False/unreliable documents or data contained in them
2. **Несоответствие критериям** — Non-compliance of the farmer or data with the criteria established by the Rules
3. **Отсутствие согласия на обработку персональных данных** — No consent for personal data processing

These are broad categories covering all the automated checks (ISJ, EASU, EGKN, etc.).

## Complete Second Dive Summary

I've now gone through all 116 pages. Here's what was missed or underappreciated in the first pass:

### NEW Finding: Subsidy Amounts Differ by Origin (2-2.7x)

| Type | Domestic | CIS/Ukraine Import | Western Import |
|------|----------|-------------------|---------------|
| Breeding cattle (meat) | 260,000₸ | 390,000₸ | 525,000₸ |
| Breeding cattle (dairy) | 350,000₸ | 390,000₸ | **700,000₸** |
| Breeding rams | 26,000₸ (domestic) | — | **260,000₸** (import) |

**Import from Western countries gets 2-2.7x MORE subsidy per animal.** This creates an incentive to import rather than buy domestic. Our scoring should consider: does the farmer strengthen local breeding industry (domestic purchase) or bring in genetic diversity (import)?

### NEW Finding: Milk Subsidies Are TIERED by Herd Size

| Herd Size | Subsidy per kg |
|-----------|---------------|
| 600+ cows | **45₸/kg** |
| 400+ cows | 30₸/kg |
| 50+ cows | 20₸/kg |
| Cooperatives | 20₸/kg |

**Larger producers get MORE per kg.** This is the government deliberately incentivizing scale-up of dairy. Our "small farmer priority" must be calibrated against this — the law already favors large dairy.

### NEW Finding: Counter-Obligation Data Is Machine-Readable in GISS

**Form 1-ВПЖ** (annual production report):
- Filled by every subsidy recipient in GISS
- Contains: production volume, price (auto-filled), gross value (auto-calculated), **deviation from previous year (auto-calculated)**
- This is the EXACT outcome data we need for Tier 2

**Form 2 (counter-obligation registry)** — **THIS IS PUBLIC DATA**:
> "Сведения о встречных обязательствах отражаются в реестре встречных обязательств по форме 2 и **являются общедоступными для пользователей**."

This means the counter-obligation compliance data is publicly visible to all GISS users. In Tier 2, we could potentially access this.

### NEW Finding: Budget Utilization Tracking (Form 1-ИСЖ)

MIOs submit annual reports to the Ministry containing:
- Per subsidy type: allocated budget, subsidized volume (target vs actual), amounts (target vs actual), remainder, **reasons for non-utilization**
- This tracks EXACTLY how budget is used per type per region
- For our budget simulator: this is the real baseline we'd compare against

### NEW Finding: Quarterly Monitoring Includes Return Amounts

> "РОСХ представляет информацию в МИО не позднее 10 числа месяца, следующего за отчётным кварталом, **включая сведения о размерах подлежащих возврату субсидий**."

The quarterly monitoring reports INCLUDE the exact amounts that need to be returned. This data exists in GISS and could feed directly into our "track record" scoring dimension in Tier 2.

### NEW Finding: Fund Return Creates Blocking Effect

> "товаропроизводитель имеет возможность подать новую заявку **только после выполнения своих предыдущих обязательств** (после возврата субсидий за несохранённое поголовье)."

Farmers who had to return funds are **BLOCKED** from new applications until they pay back. This is a binary disqualifier that the system enforces automatically.

### NEW Finding: Infrastructure Requirements Are Detailed Checklists

Приложение 5 contains **specific infrastructure requirements** per operation type:
- Dairy farms: milking equipment, cooling, milk tank, specific capacity
- Cattle farms: fencing, scales, water, livestock chute
- Poultry: automated feeding, ventilation, slaughter line, HACCP/ISO certification
- Pig farms: 200+ sow minimum, specific equipment
- Wool processing: washing equipment, drying, spinning ≥350 kg/hr

These are checked by the special commission before certain subsidy types can be approved.
